65→\tName = names.DynamicResources\n    66→\n    67→\tstateKey fwk.StateKey = Name\n    68→\n    69→\t// specialClaimInMemName is the name of the special resource claim that\n    70→\t// exists only Each claim instance is read-only because it\n    93→\t// might come from the informer cache. The instances get replaced when\n    94→\t// the plugin itself successfully does an Update.\n    95→\t//\n    96→\t// When the DRAExtendedResource feature is enabled, the special ResourceClaim\n    97→\t// which represents extended resource requests is also stored here.\n    98→\t// The plugin code should treat this field as a black box and only\n    99→\t// access it via its methods, in particular:\n   100→\t// - The all method can be used to iterate over all claims, including\n   101→\t//   the special one.\n   102→\t// - The allUserClaims method excludes the special one.\n   103→\t//\n   104→\t// Empty if the Pod has no claims and no special claim for extended\n   105→\t// resources backed by DRA, in which case the plugin has no work to do for\n   106→\t// the Pod.\n   107→\tclaims claimStore\n   108→\n   109→\t// draExtendedResource stores data for extended resources backed by DRA.\n   110→\t// It will remain empty when the DRAExtendedResource feature is disabled.\n   111→\tdraExtendedResource draExtendedResource\n   112→\n   113→\t// Allocator handles claims with structured parameters, which is all of them nowadays.\n   114→\tallocator structured.Allocator\n   115→\n   116→\t// mutex must be locked while accessing any of the fields below.\n   117→\tmutex sync.Mutex\n   118→\n   119→\t// The indices of all claims that:\n   120→\t// - are allocated\n   121→\t// - were not available on at least one node\n   122→\t//\n   123→\t// Set in parallel during Filter, so write access there must be\n   124→\t// protected by the mutex. Used by PostFilter.\n   125→\tunavailableClaims sets.Set[int]\n   126→\n   127→\t// informationsForClaim has one entry for each claim in claims.\n   128→\tinformationsForClaim []informationForClaim\n   129→\n   130→\t// nodeAllocations caches the result of Filter for the nodes, its key is node name.\n   131→\tnodeAllocations map[string]nodeAllocation\n   132→}\n   133→\n   134→func (d *stateData) Clone() fwk.StateData {\n   135→\treturn d\n   136→}\n   137→\n   138→// draExtendedResource stores data for extended resources backed by DRA.\n   139→// It will remain empty when the {\n   167→\tenabled       bool\n   168→\tfts           feature.Features\n   169→\tfilterTimeout time.Duration\n   170→\tfh            fwk.Handle\n   171→\tclientset     kubernetes.Interface\n   172→\tcelCache      *cel.Cache\n   173→\tdraManager    fwk.SharedDRAManager\n   174→}\n   175→\n   176→// New initializes a new plugin and returns it.\n   177→func New(ctx context.Context, plArgs runtime.Object, fh fwk.Handle, fts feature.Features) (fwk.Plugin, error) {\n   178→\tif !fts.EnableDynamicResourceAllocation {\n   179→\t\t// Disabled, won't do anything.\n   180→\t\treturn &DynamicResources{}, nil\n   181→\t}\n   182→\n   183→\targs, ok := plArgs.(*config.DynamicResourcesArgs)\n   184→\tif !ok {\n   185→\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"got args of type %T, want *DynamicResourcesArgs\", plArgs)\n   186→\t}\n   187→\tif err := validation.ValidateDynamicResourcesArgs(nil, args, fts); err != nil {\n   188→\t\treturn nil, err\n   189→\t}\n   190→\n   191→\tpl := &DynamicResources{\n   192→\t\tenabled:       true,\n   193→\t\tfts:           fts,\n   194→\t\tfilterTimeout: ptr.Deref(args.FilterTimeout, metav1.Duration{}).Duration,\n   195→\n   196→\t\tfh:        fh,\n   197→\t\tclientset: fh.ClientSet(),\n   198→\t\t// This is a LRU cache for compiled CEL expressions. The most\n   199→\t\t// recent 10 of them get reused across different scheduling\n   200→\t\t// cycles.\n   201→\t\tcelCache:   cel.NewCache(10, cel.Features{EnableConsumableCapacity: fts.EnableDRAConsumableCapacity}),\n   202→\t\tdraManager: fh.SharedDRAManager(),\n   203→\t}\n   204→\n   205→\treturn pl, nil\n   206→}\n   207→\n   208→var _ fwk.PreEnqueuePlugin = &DynamicResources{}\n   209→var _ fwk.PreFilterPlugin = &DynamicResources{}\n   210→var _ fwk.FilterPlugin = &DynamicResources{}\n   211→var _ fwk.PostFilterPlugin = &DynamicResources{}\n   212→var _ fwk.ReservePlugin = &DynamicResources{}\n   213→var _ fwk.EnqueueExtensions = &DynamicResources{}\n   214→var _ fwk.PreBindPlugin = &DynamicResources{}\n   215→\n   216→// Name returns name of the plugin. It is used in logs, etc.\n   217→func (pl *DynamicResources) Name() string {\n   218→\treturn Name\n   219→}\n   220→\n   221→// EventsToRegister returns the possible events that may make a Pod\n   222→// failed by this plugin schedulable.\n   223→func (pl *DynamicResources) EventsToRegister(_ context.Context) ([]fwk.ClusterEventWithHint, error) {\n   224→\tif !pl.enabled {\n   225→\t\treturn nil, nil\n   226→\t}\n   227→\t// A resource might depend on node labels for topology filtering.\n   228→\t// A new or updated node may make pods schedulable.\n   229→\t//\n   230→\t// A note about UpdateNodeTaint event:\n   231→\t// Ideally, it's supposed to register only Add | UpdateNodeLabel because UpdateNodeTaint will never change the result from this plugin.\n   232→\t// But, we may miss Node/Add event due to preCheck, and we decided to register UpdateNodeTaint | UpdateNodeLabel for all plugins registering Node/Add.\n   233→\t// See: https://github.com/kubernetes/kubernetes/issues/109437\n   234→\tnodeActionType := fwk.Add | fwk.UpdateNodeLabel | fwk.UpdateNodeTaint | fwk.UpdateNodeAllocatable\n   235→\tif pl.fts.EnableSchedulingQueueHint {\n   236→\t\t// When QHint is enabled, the problematic preCheck is already removed, and we can remove UpdateNodeTaint.\n   237→\t\tnodeActionType = fwk.Add | fwk.UpdateNodeLabel | fwk.UpdateNodeAllocatable\n   238→\t}\n   239→\n   240→\tevents := []fwk.ClusterEventWithHint{\n   241→\t\t{Event: fwk.ClusterEvent{Resource: fwk.Node, ActionType: nodeActionType}},\n   242→\t\t// Allocation is tracked in ResourceClaims, so any changes may make the pods schedulable.\n   243→\t\t{Event: fwk.ClusterEvent{Resource: fwk.ResourceClaim, ActionType: fwk.Add | fwk.Update}, QueueingHintFn: pl.isSchedulableAfterClaimChange},\n   244→\t\t// Adding the ResourceClaim name to the pod status makes pods waiting for their ResourceClaim schedulable.\n   245→\t\t{Event: fwk.ClusterEvent{Resource: fwk.Pod, ActionType: fwk.UpdatePodGeneratedResourceClaim}, QueueingHintFn: pl.isSchedulableAfterPodChange},\n   246→\t\t// A pod might be waiting for a class to get created or modified.\n   247→\t\t{Event: fwk.ClusterEvent{Resource: fwk.DeviceClass, ActionType: fwk.Add | fwk.Update}},\n   248→\t\t// Adding or updating a ResourceSlice might make a pod schedulable because new resources became available.\n   249→\t\t{Event: fwk.ClusterEvent{Resource: fwk.ResourceSlice, ActionType: fwk.Add | fwk.Update}},\n   250→\t}\n   251→\n   252→\treturn events, nil\n   253→}\n   254→\n   255→// PreEnqueue checks if there are known reasons why a pod currently cannot be\n   256→// scheduled. When this fails, one of the registered events can trigger another\n   257→// attempt.\n   258→func (pl *DynamicResources) PreEnqueue(ctx context.Context, pod *v1.Pod) (status *fwk.Status) {\n   259→\tif !pl.enabled {\n   260→\t\treturn nil\n   261→\t}\n   262→\n   263→\tif err := pl.foreachPodResourceClaim(pod, nil); err != nil {\n   264→\t\treturn statusUnschedulable(klog.FromContext(ctx), err.Error())\n   265→\t}\n   266→\treturn nil\n   267→}\n   268→\n   269→// isSchedulableAfterClaimChange is invoked for add and update claim events reported by\n   270→// an informer. It checks whether that change made a previously unschedulable\n   271→// pod schedulable. It errs on the side of letting a pod scheduling attempt\n   272→// happen. The delete claim event will not invoke it, so newObj will never be nil.\n   273→func (pl *DynamicResources) isSchedulableAfterClaimChange(logger klog.Logger, pod *v1.Pod, oldObj, newObj interface{}) (fwk.QueueingHint, error) {\n   274→\toriginalClaim, modifiedClaim, err := schedutil.As[*resourceapi.ResourceClaim](oldObj, newObj)\n   275→\tif err != nil {\n   276→\t\t// Shouldn't happen.\n   277→\t\treturn fwk.Queue, fmt.Errorf(\"unexpected object in isSchedulableAfterClaimChange: %w\", err)\n   278→\t}\n   279→\n   280→\tusesClaim := false\n   281→\tif err := pl.foreachPodResourceClaim(pod, func(_ string, claim *resourceapi.ResourceClaim) {\n   282→\t\tif claim.UID == modifiedClaim.UID {\n   283→\t\t\tusesClaim = true\n   284→\t\t}\n   285→\t}); err != nil {\n   286→\t\t// This is not an unexpected error: we know that\n   287→\t\t// foreachPodResourceClaim only returns errors for \"not\n   288→\t\t// schedulable\".\n   289→\t\tif loggerV := logger.V(6); loggerV.Enabled() {\n   290→\t\t\towner := metav1.GetControllerOf(modifiedClaim)\n   291→\t\t\tloggerV.Info(\"pod is not schedulable after resource claim change\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"claim\", klog.KObj(modifiedClaim), \"claimOwner\", owner, \"reason\", err.Error())\n   292→\t\t}\n   293→\t\treturn fwk.QueueSkip, nil\n   294→\t}\n   295→\n   296→\tif originalClaim != nil &&\n   297→\t\toriginalClaim.Status.Allocation != nil &&\n   298→\t\tmodifiedClaim.Status.Allocation == nil {\n   299→\t\t// A claim with structured parameters was deallocated. This might have made\n   300→\t\t// resources available for other pods.\n   301→\t\tlogger.V(6).Info(\"claim with structured parameters got deallocated\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"claim\", klog.KObj(modifiedClaim))\n   302→\t\treturn fwk.Queue, nil\n   303→\t}\n   304→\n   305→\tif !usesClaim {\n   306→\t\t// This was not the claim the pod was waiting for.\n   307→\t\tlogger.V(6).Info(\"unrelated claim got modified\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"claim\", klog.KObj(modifiedClaim))\n   308→\t\treturn fwk.QueueSkip, nil\n   309→\t}\n   310→\n   311→\tif originalClaim == nil {\n   312→\t\tlogger.V(5).Info(\"claim for pod got created\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"claim\", klog.KObj(modifiedClaim))\n   313→\t\treturn fwk.Queue, nil\n   314→\t}\n   315→\n   316→\t// Modifications may or may not be relevant. If the entire\n   317→\t// status is as before, then something else must have changed\n   318→\t// and we don't care. What happens in practice is that the\n   319→\t// resource driver adds the finalizer.\n   320→\tif apiequality.Semantic.DeepEqual(&originalClaim.Status, &modifiedClaim.Status) {\n   321→\t\tif loggerV := logger.V(7); loggerV.Enabled() {\n   322→\t\t\t// Log more information.\n   323→\t\t\tloggerV.Info(\"claim for pod got modified where the pod doesn't care\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"claim\", klog.KObj(modifiedClaim), \"diff\", diff.Diff(originalClaim, modifiedClaim))\n   324→\t\t} else {\n   325→\t\t\tlogger.V(6).Info(\"claim for pod got modified where the pod doesn't care\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"claim\", klog.KObj(modifiedClaim))\n   326→\t\t}\n   327→\t\treturn fwk.QueueSkip, nil\n   328→\t}\n   329→\n   330→\tlogger.V(5).Info(\"status of claim for pod got updated\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"claim\", klog.KObj(modifiedClaim))\n   331→\treturn fwk.Queue, nil\n   332→}\n   333→\n   334→// isSchedulableAfterPodChange is invoked for update pod events reported by\n   335→// an informer. It checks whether that change adds the ResourceClaim(s) that the\n   336→// pod has been waiting for.\n   337→func (pl *DynamicResources) isSchedulableAfterPodChange(logger klog.Logger, pod *v1.Pod, oldObj, newObj interface{}) (fwk.QueueingHint, error) {\n   338→\t_, modifiedPod, err := schedutil.As[*v1.Pod](nil, newObj)\n   339→\tif err != nil {\n   340→\t\t// Shouldn't happen.\n   341→\t\treturn fwk.Queue, fmt.Errorf(\"unexpected object in isSchedulableAfterClaimChange: %w\", err)\n   342→\t}\n   343→\n   344→\tif pod.UID != modifiedPod.UID {\n   345→\t\tlogger.V(7).Info(\"pod is not schedulable after change in other pod\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"modifiedPod\", klog.KObj(modifiedPod))\n   346→\t\treturn fwk.QueueSkip, nil\n   347→\t}\n   348→\n   349→\tif err := pl.foreachPodResourceClaim(modifiedPod, nil); err != nil {\n   350→\t\t// This is not an unexpected error: we know that\n   351→\t\t// foreachPodResourceClaim only returns errors for \"not\n   352→\t\t// schedulable\".\n   353→\t\tlogger.V(6).Info(\"pod is not schedulable after being updated\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod))\n   354→\t\treturn fwk.QueueSkip, nil\n   355→\t}\n   356→\n   357→\tlogger.V(5).Info(\"pod got updated and is schedulable\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod))\n   358→\treturn fwk.Queue, nil\n   359→}\n   360→\n   361→// podResourceClaims returns the ResourceClaims for all pod.Spec.PodResourceClaims.\n   362→func (pl *DynamicResources) podResourceClaims(pod *v1.Pod) ([]*resourceapi.ResourceClaim, error) {\n   363→\tclaims := make([]*resourceapi.ResourceClaim, 0, len(pod.Spec.ResourceClaims))\n   364→\tif err := pl.foreachPodResourceClaim(pod, func(_ string, claim *resourceapi.ResourceClaim) {\n   365→\t\t// We store the pointer as returned by the lister. The\n   366→\t\t// assumption is that if a claim gets modified while our code\n   367→\t\t// runs, the cache will store a new pointer, not mutate the\n   368→\t\t// existing object that we point to here.\n   369→\t\tclaims = append(claims, claim)\n   370→\t}); err != nil {\n   371→\t\treturn nil, err\n   372→\t}\n   373→\treturn claims, nil\n   374→}\n   375→\n   376→// foreachPodResourceClaim checks that each ResourceClaim for the pod exists.\n   377→// It calls an optional handler for those claims that it finds.\n   378→func (pl *DynamicResources) foreachPodResourceClaim(pod *v1.Pod, cb func(podResourceName string, claim *resourceapi.ResourceClaim)) error {\n   379→\tfor _, resource := range pod.Spec.ResourceClaims {\n   380→\t\tclaimName, mustCheckOwner, err := resourceclaim.Name(pod, &resource)\n   381→\t\tif err != nil {\n   382→\t\t\treturn err\n   383→\t\t}\n   384→\t\t// The claim name might be nil if no underlying resource claim\n   385→\t\t// was generated for the referenced claim. There are valid use\n   386→\t\t// cases when this might happen, so we simply skip it.\n   387→\t\tif claimName == nil {\n   388→\t\t\tcontinue\n   389→\t\t}\n   390→\t\tclaim, err := pl.draManager.ResourceClaims().Get(pod.Namespace, *claimName)\n   391→\t\tif err != nil {\n   392→\t\t\treturn err\n   393→\t\t}\n   394→\n   395→\t\tif claim.DeletionTimestamp != nil {\n   396→\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"resourceclaim %q is being deleted\", claim.Name)\n   397→\t\t}\n   398→\n   399→\t\tif mustCheckOwner {\n   400→\t\t\tif err := resourceclaim.IsForPod(pod, claim); err != nil {\n   401→\t\t\t\treturn err\n   402→\t\t\t}\n   403→\t\t}\n   404→\t\tif cb != nil {\n   405→\t\t\tcb(resource.Name, claim)\n   406→\t\t}\n   407→\t}\n   408→\treturn nil\n   409→}\n   410→\n   411→// hasDeviceClassMappedExtendedResource returns true when the given resource list has an extended resource, that has\n   412→// a mapping to a device class.\n   413→func hasDeviceClassMappedExtendedResource(reqs v1.ResourceList, deviceClassMapping map[v1.ResourceName]string) bool {\n   414→\tfor rName, rValue := range reqs {\n   415→\t\tif rValue.IsZero() {\n   416→\t\t\t// We only care about the resources requested by the pod we are trying to schedule.\n   417→\t\t\tcontinue\n   418→\t\t}\n   419→\t\tif schedutil.IsDRAExtendedResourceName(rName) {\n   420→\t\t\t_, ok := deviceClassMapping[rName]\n   421→\t\t\tif ok {\n   422→\t\t\t\treturn true\n   423→\t\t\t}\n   424→\t\t}\n   425→\t}\n   426→\treturn false\n   427→}\n   428→\n   429→// findExtendedResourceClaim looks for the extended resource claim, i.e., the claim with special annotation\n   430→// set to \"true\", and with the pod as owner. It must be called with all ResourceClaims in the cluster.\n   431→// The returned ResourceClaim is read-only.\n   432→func findExtendedResourceClaim(pod *v1.Pod, resourceClaims []*resourceapi.ResourceClaim) *resourceapi.ResourceClaim {\n   433→\tfor _, c := range resourceClaims {\n   434→\t\tif c.Annotations[resourceapi.ExtendedResourceClaimAnnotation] == \"true\" {\n   435→\t\t\tfor _, or := range c.OwnerReferences {\n   436→\t\t\t\tif or.Name == pod.Name && *or.Controller && or.UID == pod.UID {\n   437→\t\t\t\t\treturn c\n   438→\t\t\t\t}\n   439→\t\t\t}\n   440→\t\t}\n   441→\t}\n   442→\treturn nil\n   443→}\n   444→\n   445→// preFilterExtendedResources checks if there is any extended resource in the\n   446→// pod requests that has a device class mapping, i.e., there is a device class\n   447→// that has spec.ExtendedResourceName or its implicit extended resource name\n   448→// matching the given extended resource in that pod requests.\n   449→//\n   450→// It looks for the special resource claim for the pod created from prior scheduling\n   451→// cycle. If not found, it creates the special claim with no Requests in the Spec,\n   452→// with a temporary UID, and the specialClaimInMemName name.\n   453→// Either way, the special claim is stored in state.claims.\n   454→//\n   455→// In addition, draExtendedResource is also stored in the cycle state.\n   456→//\n   457→// It returns the special ResourceClaim and an error status. It returns nil for both\n   458→// if the feature is disabled or not required for the Pod.\n   459→func (pl *DynamicResources) preFilterExtendedResources(pod *v1.Pod, logger klog.Logger, s *stateData) (*resourceapi.ResourceClaim, *fwk.Status) {\n   460→\tif !pl.fts.EnableDRAExtendedResource {\n   461→\t\treturn nil, nil\n   462→\t}\n   463→\n   464→\tdeviceClassMapping, err := extended.DeviceClassMapping(pl.draManager)\n   465→\tif err != nil {\n   466→\t\treturn nil, statusError(logger, err, \"retrieving extended resource to DeviceClass mapping\")\n   467→\t}\n   468→\n   469→\treqs := resourcehelper.PodRequests(pod, resourcehelper.PodResourcesOptions{})\n   470→\thasExtendedResource := hasDeviceClassMappedExtendedResource(reqs, deviceClassMapping)\n   471→\tif !hasExtendedResource {\n   472→\t\treturn nil, nil\n   473→\t}\n   474→\n   475→\ts.draExtendedResource.resourceToDeviceClass = deviceClassMapping\n   476→\tr := framework.NewResource(reqs)\n   477→\ts.draExtendedResource.podScalarResources = r.ScalarResources\n   478→\n   479→\tresourceClaims, err := pl.draManager.ResourceClaims().List()\n   480→\tif err != nil {\n   481→\t\treturn nil, statusError(logger, err, \"listing ResourceClaims\")\n   482→\t}\n   483→\n   484→\t// Check if the special resource claim has been created from prior scheduling cycle.\n   485→\t//\n   486→\t// If it was already allocated earlier, that allocation might not be valid anymore.\n   487→\t// We could try to check that, but it depends on various factors that are difficult to\n   488→\t// cover (basically needs to replicate allocator logic) and if it turns out that the\n   489→\t// allocation is stale, we would have to schedule with those allocated devices not\n   490→\t// available for a new allocation. This situation should be rare (= binding failure),\n   491→\t// so we solve it via brute-force\n   492→\t// - Kick off deallocation in the background.\n   493→\t// - Mark the pod as unschedulable. Successful deallocation will make it schedulable again.\n   494→\textendedResourceClaim := findExtendedResourceClaim(pod, resourceClaims)\n   495→\tif extendedResourceClaim == nil {\n   496→\t\t// Create one special claim for all extended resources backed by DRA in the Pod.\n   497→\t\t// Create the ResourceClaim with pod as owner, with a generated name that uses\n   498→\t\t// <pod name>-extended-resources- as base. The final name will get truncated if it\n   499→\t\t// would be too long.\n   500→\t\textendedResourceClaim = &resourceapi.ResourceClaim{\n   501→\t\t\tObjectMeta: metav1.ObjectMeta{\n   502→\t\t\t\tNamespace: pod.Namespace,\n   503→\t\t\t\tName:      specialClaimInMemName,\n   504→\t\t\t\t// fake temporary UID for use in SignalClaimPendingAllocation\n   505→\t\t\t\tUID:          types.UID(uuid.NewUUID()),\n   506→\t\t\t\tGenerateName: pod.Name + \"-extended-resources-\",\n   507→\t\t\t\tOwnerReferences: []metav1.OwnerReference{\n   508→\t\t\t\t\t{\n   509→\t\t\t\t\t\tAPIVersion: \"v1\",\n   510→\t\t\t\t\t\tKind:       \"Pod\",\n   511→\t\t\t\t\t\tName:       pod.Name,\n   512→\t\t\t\t\t\tUID:        pod.UID,\n   513→\t\t\t\t\t\tController: ptr.To(true),\n   514→\t\t\t\t\t},\n   515→\t\t\t\t},\n   516→\t\t\t\tAnnotations: map[string]string{\n   517→\t\t\t\t\tresourceapi.ExtendedResourceClaimAnnotation: \"true\",\n   518→\t\t\t\t},\n   519→\t\t\t},\n   520→\t\t\tSpec: resourceapi.ResourceClaimSpec{},\n   521→\t\t}\n   522→\t}\n   523→\treturn extendedResourceClaim, nil\n   524→}\n   525→\n   526→// PreFilter invoked at the prefilter extension point to check if pod has all\n   527→// immediate claims bound. UnschedulableAndUnresolvable is returned if\n   528→// the pod cannot be scheduled at the moment on any node.\n   529→func (pl *DynamicResources) PreFilter(ctx context.Context, state fwk.CycleState, pod *v1.Pod, nodes []fwk.NodeInfo) (*fwk.PreFilterResult, *fwk.Status) {\n   530→\tif !pl.enabled {\n   531→\t\treturn nil, fwk.NewStatus(fwk.Skip)\n   532→\t}\n   533→\tlogger := klog.FromContext(ctx)\n   534→\n   535→\t// If the pod does not reference any claim, we don't need to do\n   536→\t// anything for it. We just initialize an empty state to record that\n   537→\t// observation for the other functions. This gets updated below\n   538→\t// if we get that far.\n   539→\ts := &stateData{}\n   540→\tstate.Write(stateKey, s)\n   541→\n   542→\tuserClaims, err := pl.podResourceClaims(pod)\n   543→\tif err != nil {\n   544→\t\treturn nil, statusUnschedulable(logger, err.Error())\n   545→\t}\n   546→\tlogger.V(5).Info(\"pod resource claims\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"resourceclaims\", klog.KObjSlice(userClaims))\n   547→\textendedResourceClaim, status := pl.preFilterExtendedResources(pod, logger, s)\n   548→\tif status != nil {\n   549→\t\treturn nil, status\n   550→\t}\n   551→\tclaims := newClaimStore(userClaims, extendedResourceClaim)\n   552→\n   553→\t// This check covers user and extended ResourceClaim.\n   554→\tif claims.empty() {\n   555→\t\treturn nil, fwk.NewStatus(fwk.Skip)\n   556→\t}\n   557→\n   558→\t// Counts all claims which the scheduler needs to allocate itself.\n   559→\tnumClaimsToAllocate := 0\n   560→\ts.informationsForClaim = make([]informationForClaim, claims.len())\n   561→\tfor index, claim := range claims.all() {\n   562→\t\tif claim.Status.Allocation != nil &&\n   563→\t\t\t!resourceclaim.CanBeReserved(claim) &&\n   564→\t\t\t!resourceclaim.IsReservedForPod(pod, claim) {\n   565→\t\t\t// Resource is in use. The pod has to wait.\n   566→\t\t\treturn nil, statusUnschedulable(logger, \"resourceclaim in use\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"resourceclaim\", klog.KObj(claim))\n   567→\t\t}\n   568→\n   569→\t\tif claim.Status.Allocation != nil {\n   570→\t\t\tif claim.Status.Allocation.NodeSelector != nil {\n   571→\t\t\t\tnodeSelector, err := nodeaffinity.NewNodeSelector(claim.Status.Allocation.NodeSelector)\n   572→\t\t\t\tif err != nil {\n   573→\t\t\t\t\treturn nil, statusError(logger, err)\n   574→\t\t\t\t}\n   575→\t\t\t\ts.informationsForClaim[index].availableOnNodes = nodeSelector\n   576→\t\t\t}\n   577→\t\t} else {\n   578→\t\t\tnumClaimsToAllocate++\n   579→\n   580→\t\t\t// Allocation in flight? Better wait for that\n   581→\t\t\t// to finish, see inFlightAllocations\n   582→\t\t\t// documentation for details.\n   583→\t\t\tif pl.draManager.ResourceClaims().ClaimHasPendingAllocation(claim.UID) {\n   584→\t\t\t\treturn nil, statusUnschedulable(logger, fmt.Sprintf(\"resource claim %s is in the process of being allocated\", klog.KObj(claim)))\n   585→\t\t\t}\n   586→\n   587→\t\t\t// Continue without validating the special claim for extended resource backed by DRA.\n   588→\t\t\t// For the claim template, it is not allocated yet at this point, and it does not have a spec.\n   589→\t\t\t// For the claim from prior scheduling cycle, leave it to Filter Phase to validate.\n   590→\t\t\tif claim == extendedResourceClaim {\n   591→\t\t\t\tcontinue\n   592→\t\t\t}\n   593→\n   594→\t\t\t// Check all requests and device classes. If a class\n   595→\t\t\t// does not exist, scheduling cannot proceed, no matter\n   596→\t\t\t// how the claim is being allocated.\n   597→\t\t\t//\n   598→\t\t\t// When using a control plane controller, a class might\n   599→\t\t\t// have a node filter. This is useful for trimming the\n   600→\t\t\t// initial set of potential nodes before we ask the\n   601→\t\t\t// driver(s) for information about the specific pod.\n   602→\t\t\tfor _, request := range claim.Spec.Devices.Requests {\n   603→\t\t\t\t// The requirements differ depending on whether the request has a list of\n   604→\t\t\t\t// alternative subrequests defined in the firstAvailable field.\n   605→\t\t\t\tswitch {\n   606→\t\t\t\tcase request.Exactly != nil:\n   607→\t\t\t\t\tif status := pl.validateDeviceClass(logger, request.Exactly.DeviceClassName, request.Name); status != nil {\n   608→\t\t\t\t\t\treturn nil, status\n   609→\t\t\t\t\t}\n   610→\t\t\t\tcase len(request.FirstAvailable) > 0:\n   611→\t\t\t\t\tif !pl.fts.EnableDRAPrioritizedList {\n   612→\t\t\t\t\t\treturn nil, statusUnschedulable(logger, fmt.Sprintf(\"resource claim %s, request %s: has subrequests, but the DRAPrioritizedList feature is disabled\", klog.KObj(claim), request.Name))\n   613→\t\t\t\t\t}\n   614→\t\t\t\t\tfor _, subRequest := range request.FirstAvailable {\n   615→\t\t\t\t\t\tqualRequestName := strings.Join([]string{request.Name, subRequest.Name}, \"/\")\n   616→\t\t\t\t\t\tif status := pl.validateDeviceClass(logger, subRequest.DeviceClassName, qualRequestName); status != nil {\n   617→\t\t\t\t\t\t\treturn nil, status\n   618→\t\t\t\t\t\t}\n   619→\t\t\t\t\t}\n   620→\t\t\t\tdefault:\n   621→\t\t\t\t\treturn nil, statusUnschedulable(logger, fmt.Sprintf(\"resource claim %s, request %s: unknown request type\", klog.KObj(claim), request.Name))\n   622→\t\t\t\t}\n   623→\t\t\t}\n   624→\t\t}\n   625→\t}\n   626→\n   627→\tif numClaimsToAllocate > 0 {\n   628→\t\tif loggerV := logger.V(5); loggerV.Enabled() {\n   629→\t\t\tclaimsToAllocate := make([]*resourceapi.ResourceClaim, 0, claims.len())\n   630→\t\t\tfor _, claim := range claims.toAllocate() {\n   631→\t\t\t\tclaimsToAllocate = append(claimsToAllocate, claim)\n   632→\t\t\t}\n   633→\t\t\tloggerV.Info(\"Preparing allocation with structured parameters\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"resourceclaims\", klog.KObjSlice(claimsToAllocate))\n   634→\t\t}\n   635→\n   636→\t\t// Doing this over and over again for each pod could be avoided\n   637→\t\t// by setting the allocator up once and then keeping it up-to-date\n   638→\t\t// as changes are observed.\n   639→\t\t//\n   640→\t\t// But that would cause problems for using the plugin in the\n   641→\t\t// Cluster Autoscaler. If this step here turns out to be\n   642→\t\t// expensive, we may have to maintain and update state more\n   643→\t\t// persistently.\n   644→\t\t//\n   645→\t\t// Claims (and thus their devices) are treated as \"allocated\" if they are in the assume cache\n   646→\t\t// or currently their allocation is in-flight. This does not change\n   647→\t\t// during filtering, so we can determine that once.\n   648→\t\tvar allocatedState *structured.AllocatedState\n   649→\t\tif pl.fts.EnableDRAConsumableCapacity {\n   650→\t\t\tallocatedState, err = pl.draManager.ResourceClaims().GatherAllocatedState()\n   651→\t\t\tif err != nil {\n   652→\t\t\t\treturn nil, statusError(logger, err)\n   653→\t\t\t}\n   654→\t\t\tif allocatedState == nil {\n   655→\t\t\t\treturn nil, statusError(logger, errors.New(\"nil allocated state\"))\n   656→\t\t\t}\n   657→\t\t} else {\n   658→\t\t\tallocatedDevices, err := pl.draManager.ResourceClaims().ListAllAllocatedDevices()\n   659→\t\t\tif err != nil {\n   660→\t\t\t\treturn nil, statusError(logger, err)\n   661→\t\t\t}\n   662→\t\t\tallocatedState = &structured.AllocatedState{\n   663→\t\t\t\tAllocatedDevices:         allocatedDevices,\n   664→\t\t\t\tAllocatedSharedDeviceIDs: sets.New[structured.SharedDeviceID](),\n   665→\t\t\t\tAggregatedCapacity:       structured.NewConsumedCapacityCollection(),\n   666→\t\t\t}\n   667→\t\t}\n   668→\t\tslices, err := pl.draManager.ResourceSlices().ListWithDeviceTaintRules()\n   669→\t\tif err != nil {\n   670→\t\t\treturn nil, statusError(logger, err)\n   671→\t\t}\n   672→\t\tfeatures := allocatorFeatures(pl.fts)\n   673→\t\tallocator, err := structured.NewAllocator(ctx, features, *allocatedState, pl.draManager.DeviceClasses(), slices, pl.celCache)\n   674→\t\tif err != nil {\n   675→\t\t\treturn nil, statusError(logger, err)\n   676→\t\t}\n   677→\t\ts.allocator = allocator\n   678→\t\ts.nodeAllocations = make(map[string]nodeAllocation)\n   679→\t}\n   680→\ts.claims = claims\n   681→\treturn nil, nil\n   682→}\n   683→\n   684→func allocatorFeatures(fts feature.Features) structured.Features {\n   685→\treturn structured.Features{\n   686→\t\tAdminAccess:            fts.EnableDRAAdminAccess,\n   687→\t\tPrioritizedList:        fts.EnableDRAPrioritizedList,\n   688→\t\tPartitionableDevices:   fts.EnableDRAPartitionableDevices,\n   689→\t\tDeviceTaints:           fts.EnableDRADeviceTaints,\n   690→\t\tDeviceBindingAndStatus: fts.EnableDRADeviceBindingConditions && fts.EnableDRAResourceClaimDeviceStatus,\n   691→\t\tConsumableCapacity:     fts.EnableDRAConsumableCapacity,\n   692→\t}\n   693→}\n   694→\n   695→func (pl *DynamicResources) validateDeviceClass(logger klog.Logger, deviceClassName, requestName string) *fwk.Status {\n   696→\tif deviceClassName == \"\" {\n   697→\t\treturn statusError(logger, fmt.Errorf(\"request %s: unsupported request type\", requestName))\n   698→\t}\n   699→\n   700→\t_, err := pl.draManager.DeviceClasses().Get(deviceClassName)\n   701→\tif err != nil {\n   702→\t\t// If the class cannot be retrieved, allocation cannot proceed.\n   703→\t\tif apierrors.IsNotFound(err) {\n   704→\t\t\t// Here we mark the pod as \"unschedulable\", so it'll sleep in\n   705→\t\t\t// the unscheduleable queue until a DeviceClass event occurs.\n   706→\t\t\treturn statusUnschedulable(logger, fmt.Sprintf(\"request %s: device class %s does not exist\", requestName, deviceClassName))\n   707→\t\t}\n   708→\t}\n   709→\treturn nil\n   710→}\n   711→\n   712→// PreFilterExtensions returns prefilter extensions, pod add and remove.\n   713→func (pl *DynamicResources) PreFilterExtensions() fwk.PreFilterExtensions {\n   714→\treturn nil\n   715→}\n   716→\n   717→func getStateData(cs fwk.CycleState) (*stateData, error) {\n   718→\tstate, err := cs.Read(stateKey)\n   719→\tif err != nil {\n   720→\t\treturn nil, err\n   721→\t}\n   722→\ts, ok := state.(*stateData)\n   723→\tif !ok {\n   724→\t\treturn nil, errors.New(\"unable to convert state into stateData\")\n   725→\t}\n   726→\treturn s, nil\n   727→}\n   728→\n   729→// filterExtendedResources computes the special claim's Requests based on the\n   730→// node's Allocatable. It returns the special claim updated to match what needs\n   731→// to be allocated through DRA for the node or nil if nothing needs to be allocated.\n   732→//\n   733→// It returns an error when the pod's extended resource requests cannot be allocated\n   734→// from node's Allocatable, nor matching any device class's explicit or implicit\n   735→// ExtendedResourceName.\n   736→func (pl *DynamicResources) filterExtendedResources(state *stateData, pod *v1.Pod, nodeInfo fwk.NodeInfo, logger klog.Logger) (*resourceapi.ResourceClaim, *fwk.Status) {\n   737→\textendedResourceClaim := state.claims.extendedResourceClaim()\n   738→\tif extendedResourceClaim == nil {\n   739→\t\t// Nothing to do.\n   740→\t\treturn nil, nil\n   741→\t}\n   742→\n   743→\t// The claim is from the prior scheduling cycle, return unschedulable such that it can be\n   744→\t// deleted at the PostFilter phase, and retry anew.\n   745→\tif extendedResourceClaim.Spec.Devices.Requests != nil {\n   746→\t\treturn nil, statusUnschedulable(logger, \"cannot schedule extended resource claim\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"node\", klog.KObj(nodeInfo.Node()), \"claim\", klog.KObj(extendedResourceClaim))\n   747→\t}\n   748→\n   749→\textendedResources := make(map[v1.ResourceName]int64)\n   750→\thasExtendedResource := false\n   751→\tfor rName, rQuant := range state.draExtendedResource.podScalarResources {\n   752→\t\tif !schedutil.IsDRAExtendedResourceName(rName) {\n   753→\t\t\tcontinue\n   754→\t\t}\n   755→\t\t// Skip in case request quantity is zero\n   756→\t\tif rQuant == 0 {\n   757→\t\t\tcontinue\n   758→\t\t}\n   759→\n   760→\t\tallocatable, okScalar := nodeInfo.GetAllocatable().GetScalarResources()[rName]\n   761→\t\t_, okDynamic := state.draExtendedResource.resourceToDeviceClass[rName]\n   762→\t\tif okDynamic {\n   763→\t\t\tif allocatable > 0 {\n   764→\t\t\t\t// node provides the resource via device plugin\n   765→\t\t\t\textendedResources[rName] = 0\n   766→\t\t\t} else {\n   767→\t\t\t\t// node needs to provide the resource via DRA\n   768→\t\t\t\textendedResources[rName] = rQuant\n   769→\t\t\t\thasExtendedResource = true\n   770→\t\t\t}\n   771→\t\t} else if !okScalar {\n   772→\t\t\t// has request neither provided by device plugin, nor backed by DRA,\n   773→\t\t\t// hence the pod does not fit the node.\n   774→\t\t\treturn nil, statusUnschedulable(logger, \"cannot fit resource\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"node\", klog.KObj(nodeInfo.Node()), \"resource\", rName)\n   775→\t\t}\n   776→\t}\n   777→\n   778→\t// No extended resources backed by DRA on this node.\n   779→\t// The pod may have extended resources, but they are all backed by device\n   780→\t// plugin, hence the noderesources plugin should have checked if the node\n   781→\t// can fit the pod.\n   782→\t// This dynamic resources plugin Filter phase has nothing left to do.\n   783→\tif state.claims.noUserClaim() && !hasExtendedResource {\n   784→\t\t// It cannot be allocated when reaching here, as the claim from prior scheduling cycle\n   785→\t\t// would return unschedulable earlier in this function.\n   786→\t\treturn nil, nil\n   787→\t}\n   788→\n   789→\t// Each node needs its own, potentially different variant of the claim.\n   790→\tnodeExtendedResourceClaim := extendedResourceClaim.DeepCopy()\n   791→\tnodeExtendedResourceClaim.Spec.Devices.Requests = createDeviceRequests(pod, extendedResources, state.draExtendedResource.resourceToDeviceClass)\n   792→\n   793→\tif extendedResourceClaim.Status.Allocation != nil {\n   794→\t\t// If it is already allocated, then we cannot simply allocate it again.\n   795→\t\t//\n   796→\t\t// It cannot be allocated when reaching here, as the claim found from prior scheduling cycle\n   797→\t\t// would return unschedulable earlier in this function.\n   798→\t\treturn nil, nil\n   799→\t}\n   800→\n   801→\treturn nodeExtendedResourceClaim, nil\n   802→}\n   803→\n   804→// createDeviceRequests computes the special claim's Requests based on the pod's extended resources\n   805→// that are not satisfied by the node's Allocatable.\n   806→//\n   807→// the device request name has the format: container-%d-request-%d,\n   808→// the first %d is the container's index in the pod's initContainer and containers\n   809→// the second %d is the extended resource's index in that container's sorted resource requests.\n   810→func createDeviceRequests(pod *v1.Pod, extendedResources map[v1.ResourceName]int64, deviceClassMapping map[v1.ResourceName]string) []resourceapi.DeviceRequest {\n   811→\tvar deviceRequests []resourceapi.DeviceRequest\n   812→\t// Creating the extended resource claim's Requests by\n   813→\t// iterating over the containers, and the resources in the containers,\n   814→\t// and create one request per <container, extended resource>.\n   815→\n   816→\t// pod level resources currently have only cpu and memory, they are not considered here for now.\n   817→\t// if extended resources are added to pod level resources in the future, they need to be\n   818→\t// supported separately.\n   819→\tcontainers := slices.Clone(pod.Spec.InitContainers)\n   820→\tcontainers = append(containers, pod.Spec.Containers...)\n   821→\tfor r := range extendedResources {\n   822→\t\tfor i, c := range containers {\n   823→\t\t\tcreqs := c.Resources.Requests\n   824→\t\t\tif creqs == nil {\n   825→\t\t\t\tcontinue\n   826→\t\t\t}\n   827→\t\t\tvar rQuant resource.Quantity\n   828→\t\t\tvar ok bool\n   829→\t\t\tif rQuant, ok = creqs[r]; !ok {\n   830→\t\t\t\tcontinue\n   831→\t\t\t}\n   832→\t\t\tcrq, ok := (&rQuant).AsInt64()\n   833→\t\t\tif !ok || crq == 0 {\n   834→\t\t\t\tcontinue\n   835→\t\t\t}\n   836→\t\t\tclassName, ok := deviceClassMapping[r]\n   837→\t\t\t// skip if the request does not map to a device class\n   838→\t\t\tif !ok || className == \"\" {\n   839→\t\t\t\tcontinue\n   840→\t\t\t}\n   841→\t\t\tkeys := make([]string, 0, len(creqs))\n   842→\t\t\tfor k := range creqs {\n   843→\t\t\t\tkeys = append(keys, k.String())\n   844→\t\t\t}\n   845→\t\t\t// resource requests in a container is a map, their names must\n   846→\t\t\t// be sorted to determine the resource's index order.\n   847→\t\t\tslice.SortStrings(keys)\n   848→\t\t\tridx := 0\n   849→\t\t\tfor j := range keys {\n   850→\t\t\t\tif keys[j] == r.String() {\n   851→\t\t\t\t\tridx = j\n   852→\t\t\t\t\tbreak\n   853→\t\t\t\t}\n   854→\t\t\t}\n   855→\t\t\t// i is the index of the container if the list of initContainers + containers.\n   856→\t\t\t// ridx is the index of the extended resource request in the sorted all requests in the container.\n   857→\t\t\t// crq is the quantity of the extended resource request.\n   858→\t\t\tdeviceRequests = append(deviceRequests,\n   859→\t\t\t\tresourceapi.DeviceRequest{\n   860→\t\t\t\t\tName: fmt.Sprintf(\"container-%d-request-%d\", i, ridx), // need to be container name index - extended resource name index\n   861→\t\t\t\t\tExactly: &resourceapi.ExactDeviceRequest{\n   862→\t\t\t\t\t\tDeviceClassName: className, // map external resource name -> device class name\n   863→\t\t\t\t\t\tAllocationMode:  resourceapi.DeviceAllocationModeExactCount,\n   864→\t\t\t\t\t\tCount:           crq,\n   865→\t\t\t\t\t},\n   866→\t\t\t\t})\n   867→\t\t}\n   868→\t}\n   869→\tsort.Slice(deviceRequests, func(i, j int) bool {\n   870→\t\treturn deviceRequests[i].Name < deviceRequests[j].Name\n   871→\t})\n   872→\treturn deviceRequests\n   873→}\n   874→\n   875→// Filter invoked at the filter extension point.\n   876→// It evaluates if a pod can fit due to the resources it requests,\n   877→// for both allocated and unallocated claims.\n   878→//\n   879→// For claims that are bound, then it checks that the node affinity is\n   880→// satisfied by the given node.\n   881→//\n   882→// For claims that are unbound, it checks whether the claim might get allocated\n   883→// for the node.\n   884→func (pl *DynamicResources) Filter(ctx context.Context, cs fwk.CycleState, pod *v1.Pod, nodeInfo fwk.NodeInfo) *fwk.Status {\n   885→\tif !pl.enabled {\n   886→\t\treturn nil\n   887→\t}\n   888→\tstate, err := getStateData(cs)\n   889→\tif err != nil {\n   890→\t\treturn statusError(klog.FromContext(ctx), err)\n   891→\t}\n   892→\tif state.claims.empty() {\n   893→\t\treturn nil\n   894→\t}\n   895→\n   896→\tlogger := klog.FromContext(ctx)\n   897→\tnode := nodeInfo.Node()\n   898→\tnodeExtendedResourceClaim, status := pl.filterExtendedResources(state, pod, nodeInfo, logger)\n   899→\tif status != nil {\n   900→\t\treturn status\n   901→\t}\n   902→\t// The pod has no user claim, it may have extended resource claim that is satisfied by device plugin.\n   903→\t// Then there is nothing left to do for this plugin.\n   904→\tif nodeExtendedResourceClaim == nil && state.claims.noUserClaim() {\n   905→\t\treturn nil\n   906→\t}\n   907→\n   908→\tvar unavailableClaims []int\n   909→\tfor index, claim := range state.claims.all() {\n   910→\t\tlogger.V(10).Info(\"filtering based on resource claims of the pod\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"node\", klog.KObj(node), \"resourceclaim\", klog.KObj(claim))\n   911→\n   912→\t\t// This node selector only gets set if the claim is allocated.\n   913→\t\tif nodeSelector := state.informationsForClaim[index].availableOnNodes; nodeSelector != nil && !nodeSelector.Match(node) {\n   914→\t\t\tlogger.V(5).Info(\"allocation's node selector does not match\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"node\", klog.KObj(node), \"resourceclaim\", klog.KObj(claim))\n   915→\t\t\tunavailableClaims = append(unavailableClaims, index)\n   916→\t\t\tcontinue\n   917→\t\t}\n   918→\n   919→\t\tif claim.Status.Allocation == nil {\n   920→\t\t\t// The claim is not allocated yet, don't have to check\n   921→\t\t\t// anything else.\n   922→\t\t\tcontinue\n   923→\t\t}\n   924→\n   925→\t\t// The claim is allocated, check whether it is ready for binding.\n   926→\t\tif pl.fts.EnableDRADeviceBindingConditions && pl.fts.EnableDRAResourceClaimDeviceStatus {\n   927→\t\t\tready, err := pl.isClaimReadyForBinding(claim)\n   928→\t\t\t// If the claim is not ready yet (ready false, no error) and binding has timed out\n   929→\t\t\t// or binding has failed (err non-nil), then the scheduler should consider deallocating this\n   930→\t\t\t// claim in PostFilter to unblock trying other devices.\n   931→\t\t\tif err != nil || !ready && pl.isClaimTimeout(claim) {\n   932→\t\t\t\tunavailableClaims = append(unavailableClaims, index)\n   933→\t\t\t}\n   934→\t\t}\n   935→\t}\n   936→\n   937→\t// Use allocator to check the node and cache the result in case that the node is picked.\n   938→\tvar allocations []resourceapi.AllocationResult\n   939→\tif state.allocator != nil {\n   940→\t\tallocCtx := ctx\n   941→\t\tif loggerV := logger.V(5); loggerV.Enabled() {\n   942→\t\t\tallocCtx = klog.NewContext(allocCtx, klog.LoggerWithValues(logger, \"node\", klog.KObj(node)))\n   943→\t\t}\n   944→\n   945→\t\t// Apply timeout to the operation?\n   946→\t\tif pl.fts.EnableDRASchedulerFilterTimeout && pl.filterTimeout > 0 {\n   947→\t\t\tc, cancel := context.WithTimeout(allocCtx, pl.filterTimeout)\n   948→\t\t\tdefer cancel()\n   949→\t\t\tallocCtx = c\n   950→\t\t}\n   951→\n   952→\t\t// Check which claims need to be allocated.\n   953→\t\t//\n   954→\t\t// This replaces the special ResourceClaim for extended resources with one\n   955→\t\t// matching the node.\n   956→\t\tclaimsToAllocate := make([]*resourceapi.ResourceClaim, 0, state.claims.len())\n   957→\t\textendedResourceClaim := state.claims.extendedResourceClaim()\n   958→\t\tfor _, claim := range state.claims.toAllocate() {\n   959→\t\t\tif claim == extendedResourceClaim && nodeExtendedResourceClaim != nil {\n   960→\t\t\t\tclaim = nodeExtendedResourceClaim\n   961→\t\t\t}\n   962→\t\t\tclaimsToAllocate = append(claimsToAllocate, claim)\n   963→\t\t}\n   964→\t\ta, err := state.allocator.Allocate(allocCtx, node, claimsToAllocate)\n   965→\t\tswitch {\n   966→\t\tcase errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded):\n   967→\t\t\treturn statusUnschedulable(logger, \"timed out trying to allocate devices\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"node\", klog.KObj(node), \"resourceclaims\", klog.KObjSlice(claimsToAllocate))\n   968→\t\tcase ctx.Err() != nil:\n   969→\t\t\treturn statusUnschedulable(logger, fmt.Sprintf(\"asked by caller to stop allocating devices: %v\", context.Cause(ctx)), \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"node\", klog.KObj(node), \"resourceclaims\", klog.KObjSlice(claimsToAllocate))\n   970→\t\tcase err != nil:\n   971→\t\t\t// This should only fail if there is something wrong with the claim or class.\n   972→\t\t\t// Return an error to abort scheduling of it.\n   973→\t\t\t//\n   974→\t\t\t// This will cause retries. It would be slightly nicer to mark it as unschedulable\n   975→\t\t\t// *and* abort scheduling. Then only cluster event for updating the claim or class\n   976→\t\t\t// with the broken CEL expression would trigger rescheduling.\n   977→\t\t\t//\n   978→\t\t\t// But we cannot do both. As this shouldn't occur often, aborting like this is\n   979→\t\t\t// better than the more complicated alternative (return Unschedulable here, remember\n   980→\t\t\t// the error, then later raise it again later if needed).\n   981→\t\t\treturn statusError(logger, err, \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"node\", klog.KObj(node), \"resourceclaims\", klog.KObjSlice(claimsToAllocate))\n   982→\t\t}\n   983→\t\t// Check for exact length just to be sure. In practice this is all-or-nothing.\n   984→\t\tif len(a) != len(claimsToAllocate) {\n   985→\t\t\treturn statusUnschedulable(logger, \"cannot allocate all claims\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"node\", klog.KObj(node), \"resourceclaims\", klog.KObjSlice(claimsToAllocate))\n   986→\t\t}\n   987→\t\t// Reserve uses this information.\n   988→\t\tallocations = a\n   989→\t}\n   990→\n   991→\t// Store information in state while holding the mutex.\n   992→\tif state.allocator != nil || len(unavailableClaims) > 0 {\n   993→\t\tstate.mutex.Lock()\n   994→\t\tdefer state.mutex.Unlock()\n   995→\t}\n   996→\n   997→\tif len(unavailableClaims) > 0 {\n   998→\t\t// Remember all unavailable claims. This might be observed\n   999→\t\t// concurrently, so we have to lock the state before writing.\n  1000→\n  1001→\t\tif state.unavailableClaims == nil {\n  1002→\t\t\tstate.unavailableClaims = sets.New[int]()\n  1003→\t\t}\n  1004→\n  1005→\t\tfor _, index := range unavailableClaims {\n  1006→\t\t\tstate.unavailableClaims.Insert(index)\n  1007→\t\t}\n  1008→\t\treturn statusUnschedulable(logger, \"resourceclaim not available on the node\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod))\n  1009→\t}\n  1010→\n  1011→\tif state.allocator != nil {\n  1012→\t\tstate.nodeAllocations[node.Name] = nodeAllocation{\n  1013→\t\t\tallocationResults:     allocations,\n  1014→\t\t\textendedResourceClaim: nodeExtendedResourceClaim,\n  1015→\t\t}\n  1016→\t}\n  1017→\n  1018→\treturn nil\n  1019→}\n  1020→\n  1021→// isSpecialClaimName return true when the name is the specialClaimInMemName.\n  1022→func isSpecialClaimName(name string) bool {\n  1023→\treturn name == specialClaimInMemName\n  1024→}\n  1025→\n  1026→// PostFilter checks whether there are allocated claims that could get\n  1027→// deallocated to help get the Pod schedulable. If yes, it picks one and\n  1028→// requests its deallocation.  This only gets called when filtering found no\n  1029→// suitable node.\n  1030→func (pl *DynamicResources) PostFilter(ctx context.Context, cs fwk.CycleState, pod *v1.Pod, filteredNodeStatusMap fwk.NodeToStatusReader) (*fwk.PostFilterResult, *fwk.Status) {\n  1031→\tif !pl.enabled {\n  1032→\t\treturn nil, fwk.NewStatus(fwk.Unschedulable, \"plugin disabled\")\n  1033→\t}\n  1034→\n  1035→\tlogger := klog.FromContext(ctx)\n  1036→\tstate, err := getStateData(cs)\n  1037→\tif err != nil {\n  1038→\t\treturn nil, statusError(logger, err)\n  1039→\t}\n  1040→\t// If a Pod doesn't have any resource claims attached to it, there is no need for further processing.\n  1041→\t// Thus we provide a fast path for this case to avoid unnecessary computations.\n  1042→\tif state.claims.empty() {\n  1043→\t\treturn nil, fwk.NewStatus(fwk.Unschedulable, \"no new claims to deallocate\")\n  1044→\t}\n  1045→\textendedResourceClaim := state.claims.extendedResourceClaim()\n  1046→\n  1047→\t// Iterating over a map is random. This is intentional here, we want to\n  1048→\t// pick one claim randomly because there is no better heuristic.\n  1049→\tfor index := range state.unavailableClaims {\n  1050→\t\tclaim := state.claims.get(index)\n  1051→\t\tif claim == extendedResourceClaim {\n  1052→\t\t\tif extendedResourceClaim != nil && !isSpecialClaimName(extendedResourceClaim.Name) {\n  1053→\t\t\t\t// Handled below.\n  1054→\t\t\t\tbreak\n  1055→\t\t\t}\n  1056→\t\t\tcontinue\n  1057→\t\t}\n  1058→\n  1059→\t\tif len(claim.Status.ReservedFor) == 0 ||\n  1060→\t\t\tlen(claim.Status.ReservedFor) == 1 && claim.Status.ReservedFor[0].UID == pod.UID {\n  1061→\t\t\tclaim := claim.DeepCopy()\n  1062→\t\t\tclaim.Status.ReservedFor = nil\n  1063→\t\t\tclaim.Status.Allocation = nil\n  1064→\t\t\tclaim.Status.Devices = nil\n  1065→\t\t\tlogger.V(5).Info(\"Deallocation of ResourceClaim\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"resourceclaim\", klog.KObj(claim))\n  1066→\t\t\tif _, err := pl.clientset.ResourceV1().ResourceClaims(claim.Namespace).UpdateStatus(ctx, claim, metav1.UpdateOptions{}); err != nil {\n  1067→\t\t\t\treturn nil, statusError(logger, err)\n  1068→\t\t\t}\n  1069→\t\t\treturn nil, fwk.NewStatus(fwk.Unschedulable, \"deallocation of ResourceClaim completed\")\n  1070→\t\t}\n  1071→\t}\n  1072→\n  1073→\tif extendedResourceClaim != nil && !isSpecialClaimName(extendedResourceClaim.Name) {\n  1074→\t\t// If the special resource claim for extended resource backed by DRA\n  1075→\t\t// is reserved or allocated at prior scheduling cycle, then it should be deleted.\n  1076→\t\textendedResourceClaim := extendedResourceClaim.DeepCopy()\n  1077→\t\tif err := pl.deleteClaim(ctx, extendedResourceClaim, logger); err != nil {\n  1078→\t\t\treturn nil, statusError(logger, err)\n  1079→\t\t}\n  1080→\t\treturn nil, fwk.NewStatus(fwk.Unschedulable, \"deletion of ResourceClaim completed\")\n  1081→\t}\n  1082→\treturn nil, fwk.NewStatus(fwk.Unschedulable, \"still not schedulable\")\n  1083→}\n  1084→\n  1085→// Reserve reserves claims for the pod.\n  1086→func (pl *DynamicResources) Reserve(ctx context.Context, cs fwk.CycleState, pod *v1.Pod, nodeName string) (status *fwk.Status) {\n  1087→\tif !pl.enabled {\n  1088→\t\treturn nil\n  1089→\t}\n  1090→\tstate, err := getStateData(cs)\n  1091→\tif err != nil {\n  1092→\t\treturn statusError(klog.FromContext(ctx), err)\n  1093→\t}\n  1094→\tif state.claims.empty() {\n  1095→\t\treturn nil\n  1096→\t}\n  1097→\n  1098→\tlogger := klog.FromContext(ctx)\n  1099→\n  1100→\tnumClaimsWithAllocator := 0\n  1101→\tfor _, claim := range state.claims.all() {\n  1102→\t\tif claim.Status.Allocation != nil {\n  1103→\t\t\t// Allocated, but perhaps not reserved yet. We checked in PreFilter that\n  1104→\t\t\t// the pod could reserve the claim. Instead of reserving here by\n  1105→\t\t\t// updating the ResourceClaim status, we assume that reserving\n  1106→\t\t\t// will work and only do it for real during binding. If it fails at\n  1107→\t\t\t// that time, some other pod was faster and we have to try again.\n  1108→\t\t\tcontinue\n  1109→\t\t}\n  1110→\n  1111→\t\tnumClaimsWithAllocator++\n  1112→\t}\n  1113→\n  1114→\tif numClaimsWithAllocator == 0 {\n  1115→\t\t// Nothing left to do.\n  1116→\t\treturn nil\n  1117→\t}\n  1118→\textendedResourceClaim := state.claims.extendedResourceClaim()\n  1119→\tnumClaimsToAllocate := 0\n  1120→\tneedToAllocateUserClaims := false\n  1121→\tfor _, claim := range state.claims.toAllocate() {\n  1122→\t\tnumClaimsToAllocate++\n  1123→\t\tif claim != extendedResourceClaim {\n  1124→\t\t\tneedToAllocateUserClaims = true\n  1125→\t\t}\n  1126→\t}\n  1127→\n  1128→\t// Prepare allocation of claims handled by the schedulder.\n  1129→\tif state.allocator != nil {\n  1130→\t\t// Entries in these two slices match each other.\n  1131→\t\tallocations, ok := state.nodeAllocations[nodeName]\n  1132→\t\tif !ok || len(allocations.allocationResults) == 0 {\n  1133→\t\t\t// This can happen only when claimsToAllocate has a single special claim template for extended resource backed by DRA,\n  1134→\t\t\t// But it is satisfied by the node with device plugin, hence no DRA allocation.\n  1135→\t\t\tif !needToAllocateUserClaims {\n  1136→\t\t\t\treturn nil\n  1137→\t\t\t}\n  1138→\n  1139→\t\t\t// We checked before that the node is suitable. This shouldn't have failed,\n  1140→\t\t\t// so treat this as an error.\n  1141→\t\t\treturn statusError(logger, errors.New(\"claim allocation not found for node\"))\n  1142→\t\t}\n  1143→\n  1144→\t\t// Sanity check: do we have results for all pending claims?\n  1145→\t\tif len(allocations.allocationResults) != numClaimsToAllocate ||\n  1146→\t\t\tlen(allocations.allocationResults) != numClaimsWithAllocator {\n  1147→\t\t\treturn statusError(logger, fmt.Errorf(\"internal error, have %d allocations, %d claims to allocate, want %d claims\", len(allocations.allocationResults), numClaimsToAllocate, numClaimsWithAllocator))\n  1148→\t\t}\n  1149→\n  1150→\t\tallocIndex := 0\n  1151→\t\tfor index, claim := range state.claims.toAllocate() {\n  1152→\t\t\t// The index returned is the original index in the underlying claim store, it\n  1153→\t\t\t// may not be sequentially numbered (e.g. 0, 1, 2 ...).\n  1154→\t\t\tallocation := &allocations.allocationResults[allocIndex]\n  1155→\t\t\tstate.informationsForClaim[index].allocation = allocation\n  1156→\n  1157→\t\t\tif claim == extendedResourceClaim {\n  1158→\t\t\t\t// replace the special claim template for extended\n  1159→\t\t\t\t// resource backed by DRA with the real instantiated claim.\n  1160→\t\t\t\tclaim = allocations.extendedResourceClaim\n  1161→\t\t\t}\n  1162→\n  1163→\t\t\t// Strictly speaking, we don't need to store the full modified object.\n  1164→\t\t\t// The allocation would be enough. The full object is useful for\n  1165→\t\t\t// debugging, testing and the allocator, so let's make it realistic.\n  1166→\t\t\tclaim = claim.DeepCopy()\n  1167→\t\t\tif !slices.Contains(claim.Finalizers, resourceapi.Finalizer) {\n  1168→\t\t\t\tclaim.Finalizers = append(claim.Finalizers, resourceapi.Finalizer)\n  1169→\t\t\t}\n  1170→\t\t\tclaim.Status.Allocation = allocation\n  1171→\t\t\terr := pl.draManager.ResourceClaims().SignalClaimPendingAllocation(claim.UID, claim)\n  1172→\t\t\tif err != nil {\n  1173→\t\t\t\treturn statusError(logger, fmt.Errorf(\"internal error, couldn't signal allocation for claim %s\", claim.Name))\n  1174→\t\t\t}\n  1175→\t\t\tlogger.V(5).Info(\"Reserved resource in allocation result\", \"claim\", klog.KObj(claim), \"allocation\", klog.Format(allocation))\n  1176→\t\t\tallocIndex++\n  1177→\t\t}\n  1178→\t}\n  1179→\n  1180→\treturn nil\n  1181→}\n  1182→\n  1183→// Unreserve clears the ReservedFor field for all claims.\n  1184→// It's idempotent, and does nothing if no state found for the given pod.\n  1185→func (pl *DynamicResources) Unreserve(ctx context.Context, cs fwk.CycleState, pod *v1.Pod, nodeName string) {\n  1186→\tif !pl.enabled {\n  1187→\t\treturn\n  1188→\t}\n  1189→\tstate, err := getStateData(cs)\n  1190→\tif err != nil {\n  1191→\t\treturn\n  1192→\t}\n  1193→\tif state.claims.empty() {\n  1194→\t\treturn\n  1195→\t}\n  1196→\n  1197→\tlogger := klog.FromContext(ctx)\n  1198→\n  1199→\t// we process user claims here first, extendedResourceClaim if any is handled below.\n  1200→\tfor _, claim := range state.claims.allUserClaims() {\n  1201→\t\t// If allocation was in-flight, then it's not anymore and we need to revert the\n  1202→\t\t// claim object in the assume cache to what it was before.\n  1203→\t\tif deleted := pl.draManager.ResourceClaims().RemoveClaimPendingAllocation(claim.UID); deleted {\n  1204→\t\t\tpl.draManager.ResourceClaims().AssumedClaimRestore(claim.Namespace, claim.Name)\n  1205→\t\t}\n  1206→\n  1207→\t\tif claim.Status.Allocation != nil &&\n  1208→\t\t\tresourceclaim.IsReservedForPod(pod, claim) {\n  1209→\t\t\t// Remove pod from ReservedFor. A strategic-merge-patch is used\n  1210→\t\t\t// because that allows removing an individual entry without having\n  1211→\t\t\t// the latest ResourceClaim.\n  1212→\t\t\tpatch := fmt.Sprintf(`{\"metadata\": {\"uid\": %q}, \"status\": { \"reservedFor\": [ {\"$patch\": \"delete\", \"uid\": %q} ] }}`,\n  1213→\t\t\t\tclaim.UID,\n  1214→\t\t\t\tpod.UID,\n  1215→\t\t\t)\n  1216→\t\t\tlogger.V(5).Info(\"unreserve\", \"resourceclaim\", klog.KObj(claim), \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod))\n  1217→\t\t\tclaim, err := pl.clientset.ResourceV1().ResourceClaims(claim.Namespace).Patch(ctx, claim.Name, types.StrategicMergePatchType, []byte(patch), metav1.PatchOptions{}, \"status\")\n  1218→\t\t\tif err != nil {\n  1219→\t\t\t\t// We will get here again when pod scheduling is retried.\n  1220→\t\t\t\tlogger.Error(err, \"unreserve\", \"resourceclaim\", klog.KObj(claim))\n  1221→\t\t\t}\n  1222→\t\t}\n  1223→\t}\n  1224→\n  1225→\textendedResourceClaim := state.claims.extendedResourceClaim()\n  1226→\tif extendedResourceClaim == nil {\n  1227→\t\t// there is no extended resource claim\n  1228→\t\treturn\n  1229→\t}\n  1230→\n  1231→\tif deleted := pl.draManager.ResourceClaims().RemoveClaimPendingAllocation(state.claims.getInitialExtendedResourceClaimUID()); deleted {\n  1232→\t\tpl.draManager.ResourceClaims().AssumedClaimRestore(extendedResourceClaim.Namespace, extendedResourceClaim.Name)\n  1233→\t}\n  1234→\tif isSpecialClaimName(extendedResourceClaim.Name) {\n  1235→\t\t// In memory temporary extended resource claim does not need to be deleted\n  1236→\t\treturn\n  1237→\t}\n  1238→\tlogger.V(5).Info(\"delete extended resource backed by DRA\", \"resourceclaim\", klog.KObj(extendedResourceClaim), \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"claim.UID\", extendedResourceClaim.UID)\n  1239→\textendedResourceClaim = extendedResourceClaim.DeepCopy()\n  1240→\tif err := pl.deleteClaim(ctx, extendedResourceClaim, logger); err != nil {\n  1241→\t\tlogger.Error(err, \"delete\", \"resourceclaim\", klog.KObj(extendedResourceClaim))\n  1242→\t}\n  1243→}\n  1244→\n  1245→// deleteClaim deletes the claim after removing the finalizer from the claim, if there is any.\n  1246→func (pl *DynamicResources) deleteClaim(ctx context.Context, claim *resourceapi.ResourceClaim, logger klog.Logger) error {\n  1247→\trefreshClaim := false\n  1248→\tretryErr := retry.RetryOnConflict(retry.DefaultRetry, func() error {\n  1249→\t\tif refreshClaim {\n  1250→\t\t\tupdatedClaim, err := pl.clientset.ResourceV1().ResourceClaims(claim.Namespace).Get(ctx, claim.Name, metav1.GetOptions{})\n  1251→\t\t\tif err != nil {\n  1252→\t\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"get resourceclaim %s/%s: %w\", claim.Namespace, claim.Name, err)\n  1253→\t\t\t}\n  1254→\t\t\tclaim = updatedClaim\n  1255→\t\t} else {\n  1256→\t\t\trefreshClaim = true\n  1257→\t\t}\n  1258→\t\t// Remove the finalizer to unblock removal first.\n  1259→\t\tbuiltinControllerFinalizer := slices.Index(claim.Finalizers, resourceapi.Finalizer)\n  1260→\t\tif builtinControllerFinalizer >= 0 {\n  1261→\t\t\tclaim.Finalizers = slices.Delete(claim.Finalizers, builtinControllerFinalizer, builtinControllerFinalizer+1)\n  1262→\t\t}\n  1263→\n  1264→\t\t_, err := pl.clientset.ResourceV1().ResourceClaims(claim.Namespace).Update(ctx, claim, metav1.UpdateOptions{})\n  1265→\t\tif err != nil {\n  1266→\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"update resourceclaim %s/%s: %w\", claim.Namespace, claim.Name, err)\n  1267→\t\t}\n  1268→\t\treturn nil\n  1269→\t})\n  1270→\tif retryErr != nil {\n  1271→\t\treturn retryErr\n  1272→\t}\n  1273→\n  1274→\tlogger.V(5).Info(\"Delete\", \"resourceclaim\", klog.KObj(claim))\n  1275→\terr := pl.clientset.ResourceV1().ResourceClaims(claim.Namespace).Delete(ctx, claim.Name, metav1.DeleteOptions{})\n  1276→\tif err != nil {\n  1277→\t\treturn err\n  1278→\t}\n  1279→\treturn nil\n  1280→}\n  1281→\n  1282→// PreBind gets called in a separate goroutine after it has been determined\n  1283→// that the pod should get bound to this node. Because Reserve did not actually\n  1284→// reserve claims, we need to do it now. For claims with the builtin controller,\n  1285→// we also handle the allocation.\n  1286→//\n  1287→// If anything fails, we return an error and\n  1288→// the pod will have to go into the backoff queue. The scheduler will call\n  1289→// Unreserve as part of the error handling.\n  1290→func (pl *DynamicResources) PreBind(ctx context.Context, cs fwk.CycleState, pod *v1.Pod, nodeName string) *fwk.Status {\n  1291→\tif !pl.enabled {\n  1292→\t\treturn nil\n  1293→\t}\n  1294→\tstate, err := getStateData(cs)\n  1295→\tif err != nil {\n  1296→\t\treturn statusError(klog.FromContext(ctx), err)\n  1297→\t}\n  1298→\tif state.claims.empty() {\n  1299→\t\treturn nil\n  1300→\t}\n  1301→\n  1302→\tlogger := klog.FromContext(ctx)\n  1303→\n  1304→\tfor index, claim := range state.claims.all() {\n  1305→\t\tif !resourceclaim.IsReservedForPod(pod, claim) {\n  1306→\t\t\tclaim, err := pl.bindClaim(ctx, state, index, pod, nodeName)\n  1307→\t\t\tif err != nil {\n  1308→\t\t\t\treturn statusError(logger, err)\n  1309→\t\t\t}\n  1310→\t\t\t// Updated here such that Unreserve can work with patched claim.\n  1311→\t\t\tstate.claims.set(index, claim)\n  1312→\t\t}\n  1313→\t}\n  1314→\n  1315→\tif !pl.fts.EnableDRADeviceBindingConditions || !pl.fts.EnableDRAResourceClaimDeviceStatus {\n  1316→\t\t// If we don't have binding conditions, we can return early.\n  1317→\t\t// The claim is now reserved for the pod and the scheduler can proceed with binding.\n  1318→\t\treturn nil\n  1319→\t}\n  1320→\n  1321→\t// We need to check if the device is attached to the node.\n  1322→\tneedToWait := hasBindingConditions(state)\n  1323→\n  1324→\t// If no device needs to be prepared, we can return early.\n  1325→\tif !needToWait {\n  1326→\t\treturn nil\n  1327→\t}\n  1328→\n  1329→\t// We need to wait for the device to be attached to the node.\n  1330→\tpl.fh.EventRecorder().Eventf(pod, nil, v1.EventTypeNormal, \"BindingConditionsPending\", \"Scheduling\", \"waiting for binding conditions for device on node %s\", nodeName)\n  1331→\terr = wait.PollUntilContextTimeout(ctx, 5*time.Second, time.Duration(BindingTimeoutDefaultSeconds)*time.Second, true,\n  1332→\t\tfunc(ctx context.Context) (bool, error) {\n  1333→\t\t\treturn pl.isPodReadyForBinding(state)\n  1334→\t\t})\n  1335→\tif err != nil {\n  1336→\t\tif errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded) {\n  1337→\t\t\terr = errors.New(\"device binding timeout\")\n  1338→\t\t}\n  1339→\t\t// Returning an error here causes another scheduling attempt.\n  1340→\t\t// In that next attempt, PreFilter will 1346→\t// the pod worked and we can proceed with binding it.\n  1347→\treturn nil\n  1348→}\n  1349→\n  1350→// PreBindPreFlight is called before PreBind, and determines whether PreBind is going to do 113→\t// informer callbacks. ResourceClaimTracker get added here in PreBind and removed by\n   114→\t// the informer callback (based on the \"newer than\" comparison in the\n   115→\t// assume cache).\n   116→\t//\n   117→\t// It uses cache.MetaNamespaceKeyFunc to generate object names, which\n   118→\t// therefore are \"<namespace>/<name>\".\n   119→\t//\n   120→\t// This is necessary to ensure that reconstructing the resource usage\n   121→\t// at the start of a pod scheduling cycle doesn't reuse the resources\n   122→\t// assigned to such a claim. Alternatively, claim allocation state\n   123→\t// could also get tracked across pod scheduling cycles, but that\n   124→\t// - adds complexity (need to carefully sync state with informer events\n   125→\t//   for claims and ResourceSlices)\n   126→\t// - would make integration with cluster autoscaler harder because it would need\n   127→\t//   to trigger informer callbacks.\n   128→\tcache *assumecache.AssumeCache\n   129→\t// inFlightAllocations is a map from claim UUIDs to claim objects for those claims\n   130→\t// for which allocation was triggered during a scheduling cycle and the\n   131→\t// corresponding claim status update call in PreBind has not been done\n   132→\t// yet. If another pod needs the claim, the pod is treated as \"not\n   133→\t// schedulable yet\". The cluster event for the claim status update will\n   134→\t// make it schedulable.\n   135→\t//\n   136→\t// This mechanism avoids the following problem:\n   137→\t// - Pod A triggers allocation for claim X.\n   138→\t// - Pod B shares access to that claim and gets scheduled because\n   139→\t//   the claim is assumed to be allocated.\n   140→\t// - PreBind for pod B is called first, tries to update reservedFor and\n   141→\t//   fails because the claim is not really allocated yet.\n   142→\t//\n   143→\t// We could avoid the ordering problem by allowing either pod A or pod B\n   144→\t// to set the allocation. But that is more complicated and leads to another\n   145→\t// problem:\n   146→\t// - Pod A and B get scheduled as above.\n   147→\t// - PreBind for pod A gets called first, then fails with a temporary API error.\n   148→\t//   It removes the updated claim from the assume cache because of that.\n   149→\t// - PreBind for pod B gets called next and succeeds with adding the\n   150→\t//   allocation and its own reservedFor entry.\n   151→\t// - The assume cache is now not reflecting that the claim is allocated,\n   152→\t//   which could lead to reusing the same resource for some other These are errors\n    82→\t// caused by invalid input data, like for example errors in CEL selectors, so a\n    83→\t// scheduler should abort and report that problem instead of trying to find\n    84→\t// other nodes where the error doesn't occur.\n    85→\t//\n    86→\t// In the future, special errors will be defined which enable the caller to\n    87→\t// identify which object (like claim or class) caused the problem. classLister, slices, celCache)\n   155→\t\t}\n   156→\t}\n   157→\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"internal error: no allocator available for feature set %+v, enabled allocators: %s\", features, 164→\texplicitlyEnabledAllocators = sets.New(names...)\n   165→}\n   166→\n   167→// explicitlyEnabledAllocators stores the result of EnableAllocators.\n   168→// If empty (the default), all available
