all claim info state can be updated in the reconciliation loop.\n    95→\tactivePods ActivePodsFunc\n    96→\n    97→\t// sourcesReady provides the readiness of kubelet configuration sources such as apiserver update readiness.\n    98→\t// We use it to determine when we can treat pods as inactive and react appropriately.\n    99→\tsourcesReady config.SourcesReady\n   100→\n   101→\t// KubeClient reference\n   102→\tkubeClient clientset.Interface\n   103→\n   104→\t// healthInfoCache contains cached health info\n   105→\thealthInfoCache *healthInfoCache\n   106→\n   107→\t// update channel for resource updates\n   108→\tupdate chan resourceupdates.Update\n   109→}\n   110→\n   111→// NewManager creates a new DRA manager.\n   112→//\n   113→// Most errors returned by the manager show up in the context of a pod.\n   114→// They try to adhere to the following convention:\n   115→// - Don't include the pod.\n   116→// - Use terms that are familiar to users.\n   117→// - Don't include the namespace, it can be inferred from the context.\n   118→// - Avoid repeated \"failed to ...: failed to ...\" when wrapping errors.\n   119→// - Avoid wrapping when it does not provide relevant additional information to keep the user-visible error short.\n   120→func NewManager(logger klog.Logger, kubeClient clientset.Interface, stateFileDirectory string) (*Manager, error) {\n   121→\tclaimInfoCache, err := newClaimInfoCache(logger, stateFileDirectory, draManagerStateFileName)\n   122→\tif err != nil {\n   123→\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"create ResourceClaim cache: %w\", err)\n   124→\t}\n   125→\n   126→\thealthInfoCache, err := newHealthInfoCache(filepath.Join(stateFileDirectory, \"dra_health_state\"))\n   127→\tif err != nil {\n   128→\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to create healthInfo cache: %w\", err)\n   129→\t}\n   130→\n   131→\t// TODO: for now the reconcile period is not configurable.\n   132→\t// We should consider making it configurable in the future.\n   133→\treconcilePeriod := defaultReconcilePeriod\n   134→\n   135→\tmanager := &Manager{\n   136→\t\tcache:           claimInfoCache,\n   137→\t\tkubeClient:      kubeClient,\n   138→\t\treconcilePeriod: reconcilePeriod,\n   139→\t\tactivePods:      nil,\n   140→\t\tsourcesReady:    nil,\n   141→\t\thealthInfoCache: healthInfoCache,\n   142→\t\tupdate:          make(chan resourceupdates.Update, 100),\n   143→\t}\n   144→\n   145→\treturn manager, nil\n   146→}\n   147→\n   148→func (m *Manager) NewMetricsCollector() metrics.StableCollector {\n   149→\treturn &claimInfoCollector{cache: m.cache}\n   150→}\n   151→\n   152→// GetWatcherHandler must be called after Start, it indirectly depends\n   153→// on parameters which only get passed to Start, for example the context.\n   154→func (m *Manager) GetWatcherHandler() cache.PluginHandler {\n   155→\treturn m.draPlugins\n   156→}\n   157→\n   158→// Start starts the reconcile loop of the manager.\n   159→func (m *Manager) Start(ctx context.Context, activePods ActivePodsFunc, getNode GetNodeFunc, sourcesReady config.SourcesReady) error {\n   160→\tm.initDRAPluginManager(ctx, getNode, defaultWipingDelay)\n   161→\tm.activePods = activePods\n   162→\tm.sourcesReady = sourcesReady\n   163→\tgo wait.UntilWithContext(ctx, func(ctx context.Context) { m.reconcileLoop(ctx) }, m.reconcilePeriod)\n   164→\treturn nil\n   165→}\n   166→\n   167→// initPluginManager can be used instead of Start to make the manager useable\n   168→// for calls to prepare/unprepare. It exists primarily for testing purposes.\n   169→func (m *Manager) initDRAPluginManager(ctx context.Context, getNode GetNodeFunc, wipingDelay time.Duration) {\n   170→\tm.draPlugins = draplugin.NewDRAPluginManager(ctx, m.kubeClient, getNode, m, wipingDelay)\n   171→}\n   172→\n   173→// reconcileLoop ensures that any stale state in the manager's claimInfoCache gets periodically reconciled.\n   174→func (m *Manager) reconcileLoop(ctx context.Context) {\n   175→\tlogger := klog.FromContext(ctx)\n   176→\t// Only once all sources are ready do we attempt to reconcile.\n   177→\t// This ensures that the call to m.activePods() below will succeed with\n   178→\t// the actual active pods list.\n   179→\tif m.sourcesReady == nil || !m.sourcesReady.AllReady() {\n   180→\t\treturn\n   181→\t}\n   182→\n   183→\t// Get the full list of active pods.\n   184→\tactivePods := sets.New[string]()\n   185→\tfor _, p := range m.activePods() {\n   186→\t\tactivePods.Insert(string(p.UID))\n   187→\t}\n   188→\n   189→\t// Get the list of inactive pods still referenced by any claimInfos.\n   190→\ttype podClaims struct {\n   191→\t\tuid        types.UID\n   192→\t\tnamespace  string\n   193→\t\tclaimNames []string\n   194→\t}\n   195→\tinactivePodClaims := make(map[string]*podClaims)\n   196→\tm.cache.RLock()\n   197→\tfor _, claimInfo := range m.cache.claimInfo {\n   198→\t\tfor podUID := range claimInfo.PodUIDs {\n   199→\t\t\tif activePods.Has(podUID) {\n   200→\t\t\t\tcontinue\n   201→\t\t\t}\n   202→\t\t\tif inactivePodClaims[podUID] == nil {\n   203→\t\t\t\tinactivePodClaims[podUID] = &podClaims{\n   204→\t\t\t\t\tuid:        types.UID(podUID),\n   205→\t\t\t\t\tnamespace:  claimInfo.Namespace,\n   206→\t\t\t\t\tclaimNames: []string{},\n   207→\t\t\t\t}\n   208→\t\t\t}\n   209→\t\t\tinactivePodClaims[podUID].claimNames = append(inactivePodClaims[podUID].claimNames, claimInfo.ClaimName)\n   210→\t\t}\n   211→\t}\n   212→\tm.cache.RUnlock()\n   213→\n   214→\t// Loop through all inactive pods and call UnprepareResources on them.\n   215→\tfor _, podClaims := range inactivePodClaims {\n   216→\t\tif err := m.unprepareResources(ctx, podClaims.uid, podClaims.namespace, podClaims.claimNames); err != nil {\n   217→\t\t\tlogger.Info(\"Unpreparing pod resources in reconcile loop failed, will retry\", \"podUID\", podClaims.uid, \"err\", err)\n   218→\t\t}\n   219→\t}\n   220→}\n   221→\n   222→// PrepareResources attempts to prepare all of the required resources\n   223→// for the input container, issue NodePrepareResources rpc requests\n   224→// for each new resource requirement, process their responses and update the cached\n   225→// containerResources on success.\n   226→func (m *Manager) PrepareResources(ctx context.Context, pod *v1.Pod) error {\n   227→\tstartTime := time.Now()\n   228→\terr := m.prepareResources(ctx, pod)\n   229→\tkubeletmetrics.DRAOperationsDuration.WithLabelValues(\"PrepareResources\", strconv.FormatBool(err == nil)).Observe(time.Since(startTime).Seconds())\n   230→\tif err != nil {\n   231→\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"prepare dynamic resources: %w\", err)\n   232→\t}\n   233→\treturn nil\n   234→}\n   235→\n   236→func (m *Manager) prepareResources(ctx context.Context, pod *v1.Pod) error {\n   237→\tvar err error\n   238→\tlogger := klog.FromContext(ctx)\n   239→\tbatches := make(map[*draplugin.DRAPlugin][]*drapb.Claim)\n   240→\tresourceClaims := make(map[types.UID]*resourceapi.ResourceClaim)\n   241→\n   242→\t// Do a validation pass *without* changing the claim info cache.\n   243→\t// If anything goes wrong, we don't proceed. This has the advantage\n   244→\t// that the failing pod can be deleted without getting stuck.\n   245→\t//\n   246→\t// If we added the claim and pod to the cache, UnprepareResources\n   247→\t// would have to asssume that NodePrepareResources was called and\n   248→\t// try to call NodeUnprepareResources. This is particularly bad\n   249→\t// when the driver never has been installed on the node and\n   250→\t// remains unavailable.\n   251→\tpodResourceClaims := pod.Spec.ResourceClaims\n   252→\tif utilfeature.DefaultFeatureGate.Enabled(kubefeatures.DRAExtendedResource) {\n   253→\t\tif pod.Status.ExtendedResourceClaimStatus != nil {\n   254→\t\t\textendedResourceClaim := v1.PodResourceClaim{\n   255→\t\t\t\tResourceClaimName: &pod.Status.ExtendedResourceClaimStatus.ResourceClaimName,\n   256→\t\t\t}\n   257→\t\t\tpodResourceClaims = make([]v1.PodResourceClaim, 0, len(pod.Spec.ResourceClaims)+1)\n   258→\t\t\tpodResourceClaims = append(podResourceClaims, pod.Spec.ResourceClaims...)\n   259→\t\t\tpodResourceClaims = append(podResourceClaims, extendedResourceClaim)\n   260→\t\t}\n   261→\t}\n   262→\tinfos := make([]struct {\n   263→\t\tresourceClaim *resourceapi.ResourceClaim\n   264→\t\tpodClaim      *v1.PodResourceClaim\n   265→\t\tclaimInfo     *ClaimInfo\n   266→\t\tplugins       map[string]*draplugin.DRAPlugin\n   267→\t}, len(podResourceClaims))\n   268→\tfor i := range podResourceClaims {\n   269→\t\tpodClaim := &podResourceClaims[i]\n   270→\t\tinfos[i].podClaim = podClaim\n   271→\t\tlogger.V(3).Info(\"Processing resource\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"podClaim\", podClaim.Name)\n   272→\t\tclaimName, mustCheckOwner, err := resourceclaim.Name(pod, podClaim)\n   273→\t\tif err != nil {\n   274→\t\t\treturn err\n   275→\t\t}\n   276→\n   277→\t\tif claimName == nil {\n   278→\t\t\t// Nothing to do.\n   279→\t\t\tcontinue\n   280→\t\t}\n   281→\t\t// Query claim object from the API server\n   282→\t\tresourceClaim, err := m.kubeClient.ResourceV1().ResourceClaims(pod.Namespace).Get(\n   283→\t\t\tctx,\n   284→\t\t\t*claimName,\n   285→\t\t\tmetav1.GetOptions{})\n   286→\t\tif err != nil {\n   287→\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"fetch ResourceClaim %s: %w\", *claimName, err)\n   288→\t\t}\n   289→\n   290→\t\tif mustCheckOwner {\n   291→\t\t\tif err = resourceclaim.IsForPod(pod, resourceClaim); err != nil {\n   292→\t\t\t\t// No wrapping, error is already informative.\n   293→\t\t\t\treturn err\n   294→\t\t\t}\n   295→\t\t}\n   296→\n   297→\t\t// Check if pod is in the ReservedFor for the claim\n   298→\t\tif !resourceclaim.IsReservedForPod(pod, resourceClaim) {\n   299→\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"pod %s (%s) is not allowed to use ResourceClaim %s (%s)\",\n   300→\t\t\t\tpod.Name, pod.UID, *claimName, resourceClaim.UID)\n   301→\t\t}\n   302→\n   303→\t\t// At this point we assume that we have to prepare the claim and thus need\n   304→\t\t// the driver. If the driver is currently unavailable, it is better to fail\n   305→\t\t// even if the claim is already prepared because something is wrong with\n   306→\t\t// the node.\n   307→\t\tinfos[i].resourceClaim = resourceClaim\n   308→\t\tclaimInfo, err := newClaimInfoFromClaim(resourceClaim)\n   309→\t\tif err != nil {\n   310→\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"ResourceClaim %s: %w\", resourceClaim.Name, err)\n   311→\t\t}\n   312→\t\tinfos[i].claimInfo = claimInfo\n   313→\t\tinfos[i].plugins = make(map[string]*draplugin.DRAPlugin, len(claimInfo.DriverState))\n   314→\t\tfor driverName := range claimInfo.DriverState {\n   315→\t\t\tif plugin := infos[i].plugins[driverName]; plugin != nil {\n   316→\t\t\t\tcontinue\n   317→\t\t\t}\n   318→\t\t\tplugin, err := m.draPlugins.GetPlugin(driverName)\n   319→\t\t\tif err != nil {\n   320→\t\t\t\t// No wrapping, error includes driver name already.\n   321→\t\t\t\treturn err\n   322→\t\t\t}\n   323→\t\t\tinfos[i].plugins[driverName] = plugin\n   324→\t\t}\n   325→\t}\n   326→\n   327→\t// Now that we have everything that we need, we can update the claim info cache.\n   328→\t// Almost nothing can go wrong anymore at this point.\n   329→\terr = m.cache.withLock(func() error {\n   330→\t\tfor i := range podResourceClaims {\n   331→\t\t\tresourceClaim := infos[i].resourceClaim\n   332→\t\t\tpodClaim := infos[i].podClaim\n   333→\t\t\tif resourceClaim == nil {\n   334→\t\t\t\tlogger.V(5).Info(\"No need to prepare resources, no claim generated\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"podClaim\", podClaim.Name)\n   335→\t\t\t\tcontinue\n   336→\t\t\t}\n   337→\t\t\t// Get a reference to the claim info for this claim from the cache.\n   338→\t\t\t// If there isn't one yet, then add it to the cache.\n   339→\t\t\tclaimInfo, exists := m.cache.get(resourceClaim.Name, resourceClaim.Namespace)\n   340→\t\t\tif !exists {\n   341→\t\t\t\tclaimInfo = infos[i].claimInfo\n   342→\t\t\t\tm.cache.add(claimInfo)\n   343→\t\t\t\tlogger.V(6).Info(\"Created new claim info cache entry\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"podClaim\", podClaim.Name, \"claim\", klog.KObj(resourceClaim), \"claimInfoEntry\", claimInfo)\n   344→\t\t\t} else {\n   345→\t\t\t\tif claimInfo.ClaimUID != resourceClaim.UID {\n   346→\t\t\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"old ResourceClaim with same name %s and different UID %s still exists (previous pod force-deleted?!)\", resourceClaim.Name, claimInfo.ClaimUID)\n   347→\t\t\t\t}\n   348→\t\t\t\tlogger.V(6).Info(\"Found existing claim info cache entry\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"podClaim\", podClaim.Name, \"claim\", klog.KObj(resourceClaim), \"claimInfoEntry\", claimInfo)\n   349→\t\t\t}\n   350→\n   351→\t\t\t// Add a reference to the current pod in the claim info.\n   352→\t\t\tclaimInfo.addPodReference(pod.UID)\n   353→\n   354→\t\t\t// Checkpoint to ensure all claims we plan to prepare are tracked.\n   355→\t\t\t// If something goes wrong and the newly referenced pod gets\n   356→\t\t\t// deleted without a successful prepare call, we will catch\n   357→\t\t\t// that in the reconcile loop and take the appropriate action.\n   358→\t\t\tif err := m.cache.syncToCheckpoint(); err != nil {\n   359→\t\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"checkpoint ResourceClaim cache: %w\", err)\n   360→\t\t\t}\n   361→\n   362→\t\t\t// If this claim is already prepared, there is no need to prepare it again.\n   363→\t\t\tif claimInfo.isPrepared() {\n   364→\t\t\t\tlogger.V(5).Info(\"Resources already prepared\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"podClaim\", podClaim.Name, \"claim\", klog.KObj(resourceClaim))\n   365→\t\t\t\treturn nil\n   366→\t\t\t}\n   367→\n   368→\t\t\t// This saved claim will be used to update ClaimInfo cache\n   369→\t\t\t// after NodePrepareResources GRPC succeeds\n   370→\t\t\tresourceClaims[claimInfo.ClaimUID] = resourceClaim\n   371→\n   372→\t\t\t// Loop through all drivers and prepare for calling NodePrepareResources.\n   373→\t\t\tclaim := &drapb.Claim{\n   374→\t\t\t\tNamespace: claimInfo.Namespace,\n   375→\t\t\t\tUid:       string(claimInfo.ClaimUID),\n   376→\t\t\t\tName:      claimInfo.ClaimName,\n   377→\t\t\t}\n   378→\t\t\tfor driverName := range claimInfo.DriverState {\n   379→\t\t\t\tplugin := infos[i].plugins[driverName]\n   380→\t\t\t\tbatches[plugin] = append(batches[plugin], claim)\n   381→\t\t\t}\n   382→\t\t}\n   383→\n   384→\t\treturn nil\n   385→\t})\n   386→\tif err != nil {\n   387→\t\t// No error wrapping because there is no additional context needed.\n   388→\t\t// What we get here are the errors from our own callback above.\n   389→\t\treturn err\n   390→\t}\n   391→\n   392→\t// Call NodePrepareResources for all claims in each batch.\n   393→\t// If there is any error, processing gets aborted.\n   394→\t// We could try to continue, but that would make the code more complex.\n   395→\tfor plugin, claims := range batches {\n   396→\t\t// Call NodePrepareResources RPC for all resource handles.\n   397→\t\tresponse, err := plugin.NodePrepareResources(ctx, &drapb.NodePrepareResourcesRequest{Claims: claims})\n   398→\t\tif err != nil {\n   399→\t\t\t// General error unrelated to any particular claim.\n   400→\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"NodePrepareResources: %w\", err)\n   401→\t\t}\n   402→\t\tfor claimUID, result := range response.Claims {\n   403→\t\t\treqClaim := lookupClaimRequest(claims, claimUID)\n   404→\t\t\tif reqClaim == nil {\n   405→\t\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"NodePrepareResources returned result for unknown claim UID %s\", claimUID)\n   406→\t\t\t}\n   407→\t\t\tif result.GetError() != \"\" {\n   408→\t\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"NodePrepareResources failed for ResourceClaim %s: %s\", reqClaim.Name, result.Error)\n   409→\t\t\t}\n   410→\n   411→\t\t\tclaim := resourceClaims[types.UID(claimUID)]\n   412→\n   413→\t\t\t// Add the prepared CDI devices to the claim info\n   414→\t\t\terr := m.cache.withLock(func() error {\n   415→\t\t\t\tinfo, exists := m.cache.get(claim.Name, claim.Namespace)\n   416→\t\t\t\tif !exists {\n   417→\t\t\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"internal error: unable to get claim info for ResourceClaim %s\", claim.Name)\n   418→\t\t\t\t}\n   419→\t\t\t\tfor _, device := range result.GetDevices() {\n   420→\t\t\t\t\tinfo.addDevice(plugin.DriverName(), state.Device{PoolName: device.PoolName, DeviceName: device.DeviceName, RequestNames: device.RequestNames, CDIDeviceIDs: device.CdiDeviceIds})\n   421→\t\t\t\t}\n   422→\t\t\t\treturn nil\n   423→\t\t\t})\n   424→\t\t\tif err != nil {\n   425→\t\t\t\t// No wrapping, this is the error above.\n   426→\t\t\t\treturn err\n   427→\t\t\t}\n   428→\t\t}\n   429→\n   430→\t\tunfinished := len(claims) - len(response.Claims)\n   431→\t\tif unfinished != 0 {\n   432→\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"NodePrepareResources skipped %d ResourceClaims\", unfinished)\n   433→\t\t}\n   434→\t}\n   435→\n   436→\t// Atomically perform some operations on the claimInfo cache.\n   437→\terr = m.cache.withLock(func() error {\n   438→\t\t// Mark all pod claims as prepared.\n   439→\t\tfor _, claim := range resourceClaims {\n   440→\t\t\tinfo, exists := m.cache.get(claim.Name, claim.Namespace)\n   441→\t\t\tif !exists {\n   442→\t\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"internal error: unable to get claim info for ResourceClaim %s\", claim.Name)\n   443→\t\t\t}\n   444→\t\t\tinfo.setPrepared()\n   445→\t\t}\n   446→\n   447→\t\t// Checkpoint to ensure all prepared claims are tracked with their list\n   448→\t\t// of CDI devices attached.\n   449→\t\tif err := m.cache.syncToCheckpoint(); err != nil {\n   450→\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"checkpoint ResourceClaim state: %w\", err)\n   451→\t\t}\n   452→\n   453→\t\treturn nil\n   454→\t})\n   455→\tif err != nil {\n   456→\t\t// No wrapping, this is the error above.\n   457→\t\treturn err\n   458→\t}\n   459→\n   460→\treturn nil\n   461→}\n   462→\n   463→func lookupClaimRequest(claims []*drapb.Claim, claimUID string) *drapb.Claim {\n   464→\tfor _, claim := range claims {\n   465→\t\tif claim.Uid == claimUID {\n   466→\t\t\treturn claim\n   467→\t\t}\n   468→\t}\n   469→\treturn nil\n   470→}\n   471→\n   472→// GetResources gets a ContainerInfo object from the claimInfo cache.\n   473→// This information is used by the caller to update a container config.\n   474→func (m *Manager) GetResources(pod *v1.Pod, container *v1.Container) (*ContainerInfo, error) {\n   475→\tcdiDevices := []kubecontainer.CDIDevice{}\n   476→\n   477→\tfor i := range pod.Spec.ResourceClaims {\n   478→\t\tpodClaim := &pod.Spec.ResourceClaims[i]\n   479→\t\tclaimName, _, err := resourceclaim.Name(pod, podClaim)\n   480→\t\tif err != nil {\n   481→\t\t\t// No wrapping, error is already informative.\n   482→\t\t\treturn nil, err\n   483→\t\t}\n   484→\t\t// The claim name might be nil if no underlying resource claim\n   485→\t\t// was generated for the referenced claim. There are valid use\n   486→\t\t// cases when this might happen, so we simply skip it.\n   487→\t\tif claimName == nil {\n   488→\t\t\tcontinue\n   489→\t\t}\n   490→\t\tfor _, claim := range container.Resources.Claims {\n   491→\t\t\tif podClaim.Name != claim.Name {\n   492→\t\t\t\tcontinue\n   493→\t\t\t}\n   494→\n   495→\t\t\terr := m.cache.withRLock(func() error {\n   496→\t\t\t\tclaimInfo, exists := m.cache.get(*claimName, pod.Namespace)\n   497→\t\t\t\tif !exists {\n   498→\t\t\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"internal error: unable to get claim info for ResourceClaim %s\", *claimName)\n   499→\t\t\t\t}\n   500→\n   501→\t\t\t\t// As of Kubernetes 1.31, CDI device IDs are not passed via annotations anymore.\n   502→\t\t\t\tcdiDevices = append(cdiDevices, claimInfo.cdiDevicesAsList(claim.Request)...)\n   503→\n   504→\t\t\t\treturn nil\n   505→\t\t\t})\n   506→\t\t\tif err != nil {\n   507→\t\t\t\t// No wrapping, this is the error above.\n   508→\t\t\t\treturn nil, err\n   509→\t\t\t}\n   510→\t\t}\n   511→\t}\n   512→\n   513→\tif utilfeature.DefaultFeatureGate.Enabled(kubefeatures.DRAExtendedResource) && pod.Status.ExtendedResourceClaimStatus != nil {\n   514→\t\tclaimName := pod.Status.ExtendedResourceClaimStatus.ResourceClaimName\n   515→\t\t// if the container has requests for extended resources backed by DRA,\n   516→\t\t// they must have been allocated via the extendedResourceClaim created\n   517→\t\t// by the kube-scheduler.\n   518→\t\terr := m.cache.withRLock(func() error {\n   519→\t\t\tclaimInfo, exists := m.cache.get(claimName, pod.Namespace)\n   520→\t\t\tif !exists {\n   521→\t\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"unable to get claim info for claim %s in namespace %s\", claimName, pod.Namespace)\n   522→\t\t\t}\n   523→\n   524→\t\t\tfor rName, rValue := range container.Resources.Requests {\n   525→\t\t\t\tif rValue.IsZero() {\n   526→\t\t\t\t\t// We only care about the resources requested by the pod\n   527→\t\t\t\t\tcontinue\n   528→\t\t\t\t}\n   529→\t\t\t\tif schedutil.IsDRAExtendedResourceName(rName) {\n   530→\t\t\t\t\trequestName := \"\"\n   531→\t\t\t\t\tfor _, rm := range pod.Status.ExtendedResourceClaimStatus.RequestMappings {\n   532→\t\t\t\t\t\tif rm.ContainerName == container.Name && rm.ResourceName == rName.String() {\n   533→\t\t\t\t\t\t\trequestName = rm.RequestName\n   534→\t\t\t\t\t\t\tbreak\n   535→\t\t\t\t\t\t}\n   536→\t\t\t\t\t}\n   537→\t\t\t\t\tif requestName != \"\" {\n   538→\t\t\t\t\t\t// As of Kubernetes 1.31, CDI device IDs are not passed via annotations anymore.\n   539→\t\t\t\t\t\tcdiDevices = append(cdiDevices, claimInfo.cdiDevicesAsList(requestName)...)\n   540→\t\t\t\t\t}\n   541→\t\t\t\t}\n   542→\t\t\t}\n   543→\t\t\treturn nil\n   544→\t\t})\n   545→\t\tif err != nil {\n   546→\t\t\treturn nil, err\n   547→\t\t}\n   548→\t}\n   549→\treturn &ContainerInfo{CDIDevices: cdiDevices}, nil\n   550→}\n   551→\n   552→// UnprepareResources calls a driver's NodeUnprepareResource API for each resource claim owned by a pod.\n   553→// This function is idempotent and may be called multiple times against the same pod.\n   554→// As such, calls to the underlying NodeUnprepareResource API are skipped for claims that have\n   555→// already been successfully unprepared.\n   556→func (m *Manager) UnprepareResources(ctx context.Context, pod *v1.Pod) error {\n   557→\tstartTime := time.Now()\n   558→\terr := m.unprepareResourcesForPod(ctx, pod)\n   559→\tkubeletmetrics.DRAOperationsDuration.WithLabelValues(\"UnprepareResources\", strconv.FormatBool(err == nil)).Observe(time.Since(startTime).Seconds())\n   560→\tif err != nil {\n   561→\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"unprepare dynamic resources: %w\", err)\n   562→\t}\n   563→\treturn nil\n   564→}\n   565→\n   566→func (m *Manager) unprepareResourcesForPod(ctx context.Context, pod *v1.Pod) error {\n   567→\tvar claimNames []string\n   568→\tfor i := range pod.Spec.ResourceClaims {\n   569→\t\tclaimName, _, err := resourceclaim.Name(pod, &pod.Spec.ResourceClaims[i])\n   570→\t\tif err != nil {\n   571→\t\t\t// No wrapping, the error is already informative.\n   572→\t\t\treturn err\n   573→\t\t}\n   574→\t\t// The claim name might be nil if no underlying resource claim\n   575→\t\t// was generated for the referenced claim. There are valid use\n   576→\t\t// cases when this might happen, so we simply skip it.\n   577→\t\tif claimName == nil {\n   578→\t\t\tcontinue\n   579→\t\t}\n   580→\t\tclaimNames = append(claimNames, *claimName)\n   581→\t}\n   582→\tif utilfeature.DefaultFeatureGate.Enabled(kubefeatures.DRAExtendedResource) {\n   583→\t\tif pod.Status.ExtendedResourceClaimStatus != nil {\n   584→\t\t\tclaimNames = append(claimNames, pod.Status.ExtendedResourceClaimStatus.ResourceClaimName)\n   585→\t\t}\n   586→\t}\n   587→\n   588→\treturn m.unprepareResources(ctx, pod.UID, pod.Namespace, claimNames)\n   589→}\n   590→\n   591→func (m *Manager) unprepareResources(ctx context.Context, podUID types.UID, namespace string, claimNames []string) error {\n   592→\tlogger := klog.FromContext(ctx)\n   593→\tbatches := make(map[string][]*drapb.Claim)\n   594→\tclaimNamesMap := make(map[types.UID]string)\n   595→\tfor _, claimName := range claimNames {\n   596→\t\t// Atomically perform some operations on the claimInfo cache.\n   597→\t\terr := m.cache.withLock(func() error {\n   598→\t\t\t// Get the claim info from the cache\n   599→\t\t\tclaimInfo, exists := m.cache.get(claimName, namespace)\n   600→\n   601→\t\t\t// Skip calling NodeUnprepareResource if claim info is not cached\n   602→\t\t\tif !exists {\n   603→\t\t\t\treturn nil\n   604→\t\t\t}\n   605→\n   606→\t\t\t// Skip calling NodeUnprepareResource if other pods are still referencing it\n   607→\t\t\tif len(claimInfo.PodUIDs) > 1 {\n   608→\t\t\t\t// We delay checkpointing of this change until\n   609→\t\t\t\t// UnprepareResources returns successfully. It is OK to do\n   610→\t\t\t\t// this because we will only return successfully from this call\n   611→\t\t\t\t// if the checkpoint has succeeded. That means if the kubelet\n   612→\t\t\t\t// is ever restarted before this checkpoint succeeds, we will\n   613→\t\t\t\t// simply call into this (idempotent) function again.\n   614→\t\t\t\tclaimInfo.deletePodReference(podUID)\n   615→\t\t\t\treturn nil\n   616→\t\t\t}\n   617→\n   618→\t\t\t// This claimInfo name will be used to update ClaimInfo cache\n   619→\t\t\t// after NodeUnprepareResources GRPC succeeds\n   620→\t\t\tclaimNamesMap[claimInfo.ClaimUID] = claimInfo.ClaimName\n   621→\n   622→\t\t\t// Loop through all drivers and prepare for calling NodeUnprepareResources.\n   623→\t\t\tclaim := &drapb.Claim{\n   624→\t\t\t\tNamespace: claimInfo.Namespace,\n   625→\t\t\t\tUid:       string(claimInfo.ClaimUID),\n   626→\t\t\t\tName:      claimInfo.ClaimName,\n   627→\t\t\t}\n   628→\t\t\tfor driverName := range claimInfo.DriverState {\n   629→\t\t\t\tbatches[driverName] = append(batches[driverName], claim)\n   630→\t\t\t}\n   631→\n   632→\t\t\treturn nil\n   633→\t\t})\n   634→\t\tif err != nil {\n   635→\t\t\t// No wrapping, this is the error above.\n   636→\t\t\treturn err\n   637→\t\t}\n   638→\t}\n   639→\n   640→\t// Call NodeUnprepareResources for all claims in each batch.\n   641→\t// If there is any error, processing gets aborted.\n   642→\t// We could try to continue, but that would make the code more complex.\n   643→\tfor driverName, claims := range batches {\n   644→\t\t// Call NodeUnprepareResources RPC for all resource handles.\n   645→\t\tplugin, err := m.draPlugins.GetPlugin(driverName)\n   646→\t\tif plugin == nil {\n   647→\t\t\t// No wrapping, error includes driver name already.\n   648→\t\t\treturn err\n   649→\t\t}\n   650→\t\tresponse, err := plugin.NodeUnprepareResources(ctx, &drapb.NodeUnprepareResourcesRequest{Claims: claims})\n   651→\t\tif err != nil {\n   652→\t\t\t// General error unrelated to any particular claim.\n   653→\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"NodeUnprepareResources: %w\", err)\n   654→\t\t}\n   655→\n   656→\t\tfor claimUID, result := range response.Claims {\n   657→\t\t\treqClaim := lookupClaimRequest(claims, claimUID)\n   658→\t\t\tif reqClaim == nil {\n   659→\t\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"NodeUnprepareResources returned result for unknown claim UID %s\", claimUID)\n   660→\t\t\t}\n   661→\t\t\tif result.GetError() != \"\" {\n   662→\t\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"NodeUnprepareResources failed for ResourceClaim %s: %s\", reqClaim.Name, result.Error)\n   663→\t\t\t}\n   664→\t\t}\n   665→\n   666→\t\tunfinished := len(claims) - len(response.Claims)\n   667→\t\tif unfinished != 0 {\n   668→\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"NodeUnprepareResources skipped %d ResourceClaims\", unfinished)\n   669→\t\t}\n   670→\t}\n   671→\n   672→\t// Atomically perform some operations on the claimInfo cache.\n   673→\terr := m.cache.withLock(func() error {\n   674→\t\t// TODO(#132978): Re-evaluate this logic to support post-mortem health updates.\n   675→\t\t// As of the initial implementation, we immediately delete the claim info upon\n   676→\t\t// unprepare. This means a late-arriving health update for a terminated pod\n   677→\t\t// will be missed. A future enhancement could be to \"tombstone\" this entry for\n   678→\t\t// a grace period instead of deleting it.\n   679→\n   680→\t\t// Delete all claimInfos from the cache that have just been unprepared.\n   681→\t\tfor _, claimName := range claimNamesMap {\n   682→\t\t\tclaimInfo, _ := m.cache.get(claimName, namespace)\n   683→\t\t\tm.cache.delete(claimName, namespace)\n   684→\t\t\tlogger.V(6).Info(\"Deleted claim info cache entry\", \"claim\", klog.KRef(namespace, claimName), \"claimInfoEntry\", claimInfo)\n   685→\t\t}\n   686→\n   687→\t\t// Atomically sync the cache back to the checkpoint.\n   688→\t\tif err := m.cache.syncToCheckpoint(); err != nil {\n   689→\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"checkpoint ResourceClaim state: %w\", err)\n   690→\t\t}\n   691→\t\treturn nil\n   692→\t})\n   693→\tif err != nil {\n   694→\t\t// No wrapping, this is the error above.\n   695→\t\treturn err\n   696→\t}\n   697→\n   698→\treturn nil\n   699→}\n   700→\n   701→// PodMightNeedToUnprepareResources returns true if the pod might need to\n   702→// unprepare resources\n   703→func (m *Manager) PodMightNeedToUnprepareResources(uid types.UID) bool {\n   704→\tm.cache.Lock()\n   705→\tdefer m.cache.Unlock()\n   706→\treturn m.cache.hasPodReference(uid)\n   707→}\n   708→\n   709→// GetContainerClaimInfos gets Container's ClaimInfo\n   710→func (m *Manager) GetContainerClaimInfos(pod *v1.Pod, container *v1.Container) ([]*ClaimInfo, error) {\n   711→\tclaimInfos := make([]*ClaimInfo, 0, len(pod.Spec.ResourceClaims))\n   712→\n   713→\tfor i, podResourceClaim := range pod.Spec.ResourceClaims {\n   714→\t\tclaimName, _, err := resourceclaim.Name(pod, &pod.Spec.ResourceClaims[i])\n   715→\t\tif err != nil {\n   716→\t\t\t// No wrapping, the error is already informative.\n   717→\t\t\treturn nil, err\n   718→\t\t}\n   719→\n   720→\t\tif claimName == nil {\n   721→\t\t\t// No ResourceClaim needed.\n   722→\t\t\tcontinue\n   723→\t\t}\n   724→\n   725→\t\t// Ownership doesn't get checked here, this should have been done before.\n   726→\n   727→\t\tfor _, claim := range container.Resources.Claims {\n   728→\t\t\tif podResourceClaim.Name != claim.Name {\n   729→\t\t\t\tcontinue\n   730→\t\t\t}\n   731→\n   732→\t\t\terr := m.cache.withRLock(func() error {\n   733→\t\t\t\tclaimInfo, exists := m.cache.get(*claimName, pod.Namespace)\n   734→\t\t\t\tif !exists {\n   735→\t\t\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"unable to get information for ResourceClaim %s\", *claimName)\n   736→\t\t\t\t}\n   737→\t\t\t\tclaimInfos = append(claimInfos, claimInfo.DeepCopy())\n   738→\t\t\t\treturn nil\n   739→\t\t\t})\n   740→\t\t\tif err != nil {\n   741→\t\t\t\t// No wrapping, this is the error above.\n   742→\t\t\t\treturn nil, err\n   743→\t\t\t}\n   744→\t\t}\n   745→\t}\n   746→\n   747→\tif utilfeature.DefaultFeatureGate.Enabled(kubefeatures.DRAExtendedResource) {\n   748→\t\t// Handle the special claim for extended resources backed by DRA in the pod\n   749→\t\tif pod.Status.ExtendedResourceClaimStatus != nil {\n   750→\t\t\tvar hasExtendedResourceClaim bool\n   751→\t\t\tfor _, n := range pod.Status.ExtendedResourceClaimStatus.RequestMappings {\n   752→\t\t\t\tif n.ContainerName == container.Name {\n   753→\t\t\t\t\thasExtendedResourceClaim = true\n   754→\t\t\t\t\tbreak\n   755→\t\t\t\t}\n   756→\t\t\t}\n   757→\t\t\tif !hasExtendedResourceClaim {\n   758→\t\t\t\treturn claimInfos, nil\n   759→\t\t\t}\n   760→\t\t\tclaimName := &pod.Status.ExtendedResourceClaimStatus.ResourceClaimName\n   761→\t\t\terr := m.cache.withRLock(func() error {\n   762→\t\t\t\tclaimInfo, exists := m.cache.get(*claimName, pod.Namespace)\n   763→\t\t\t\tif !exists {\n   764→\t\t\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"unable to get claim info for claim %s in namespace %s\", *claimName, pod.Namespace)\n   765→\t\t\t\t}\n   766→\t\t\t\tclaimInfos = append(claimInfos, claimInfo.DeepCopy())\n   767→\t\t\t\treturn nil\n   768→\t\t\t})\n   769→\t\t\tif err != nil {\n   770→\t\t\t\t// No wrapping, this is the error above.\n   771→\t\t\t\treturn nil, err\n   772→\t\t\t}\n   773→\t\t}\n   774→\t}\n   775→\treturn claimInfos, nil\n   776→}\n   777→\n   778→// UpdateAllocatedResourcesStatus updates the health status of allocated DRA resources in the pod's container statuses.\n   779→func (m *Manager) UpdateAllocatedResourcesStatus(pod *v1.Pod, status *v1.PodStatus) {\n   780→\tlogger := klog.FromContext(context.Background())\n   781→\tfor _, container := range pod.Spec.Containers {\n   782→\t\t// Get all the DRA claim details associated with this specific container.\n   783→\t\tclaimInfos, err := m.GetContainerClaimInfos(pod, &container)\n   784→\t\tif err != nil {\n   785→\t\t\tlogger.Error(err, \"Failed to get claim infos for container\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"container\", container.Name)\n   786→\t\t\tcontinue\n   787→\t\t}\n   788→\n   789→\t\t// Find the corresponding container status\n   790→\t\tfor i, containerStatus := range status.ContainerStatuses {\n   791→\t\t\tif containerStatus.Name != container.Name {\n   792→\t\t\t\tcontinue\n   793→\t\t\t}\n   794→\n   795→\t\t\t// Ensure the slice exists. Use a map for efficient updates by resource name.\n   796→\t\t\tresourceStatusMap := make(map[v1.ResourceName]*v1.ResourceStatus)\n   797→\t\t\tif status.ContainerStatuses[i].AllocatedResourcesStatus != nil {\n   798→\t\t\t\tfor idx := range status.ContainerStatuses[i].AllocatedResourcesStatus {\n   799→\t\t\t\t\t// Store pointers to modify in place\n   800→\t\t\t\t\tresourceStatusMap[status.ContainerStatuses[i].AllocatedResourcesStatus[idx].Name] = &status.ContainerStatuses[i].AllocatedResourcesStatus[idx]\n   801→\t\t\t\t}\n   802→\t\t\t} else {\n   803→\t\t\t\tstatus.ContainerStatuses[i].AllocatedResourcesStatus = []v1.ResourceStatus{}\n   804→\t\t\t}\n   805→\n   806→\t\t\t// Loop through each claim associated with the container\n   807→\t\t\tfor _, claimInfo := range claimInfos {\n   808→\t\t\t\tvar resourceName v1.ResourceName\n   809→\t\t\t\tfoundClaimInSpec := false\n   810→\t\t\t\tfor _, cClaim := range container.Resources.Claims {\n   811→\t\t\t\t\tif cClaim.Name == claimInfo.ClaimName {\n   812→\t\t\t\t\t\tif cClaim.Request == \"\" {\n   813→\t\t\t\t\t\t\tresourceName = v1.ResourceName(fmt.Sprintf(\"claim:%s\", cClaim.Name))\n   814→\t\t\t\t\t\t} else {\n   815→\t\t\t\t\t\t\tresourceName = v1.ResourceName(fmt.Sprintf(\"claim:%s/%s\", cClaim.Name, cClaim.Request))\n   816→\t\t\t\t\t\t}\n   817→\t\t\t\t\t\tfoundClaimInSpec = true\n   818→\t\t\t\t\t\tbreak\n   819→\t\t\t\t\t}\n   820→\t\t\t\t}\n   821→\t\t\t\tif !foundClaimInSpec {\n   822→\t\t\t\t\tlogger.V(4).Info(\"Could not find matching resource claim in container spec\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod), \"container\", container.Name, \"claimName\", claimInfo.ClaimName)\n   823→\t\t\t\t\tcontinue\n   824→\t\t\t\t}\n   825→\n   826→\t\t\t\t// Get or create the ResourceStatus entry for this claim\n   827→\t\t\t\tresStatus, ok := resourceStatusMap[resourceName]\n   828→\n   829→\t\t\t\tif !ok {\n   830→\t\t\t\t\t// Create a new entry and add it to the map and the slice\n   831→\t\t\t\t\tnewStatus := v1.ResourceStatus{\n   832→\t\t\t\t\t\tName:      resourceName,\n   833→\t\t\t\t\t\tResources: []v1.ResourceHealth{},\n   834→\t\t\t\t\t}\n   835→\t\t\t\t\tstatus.ContainerStatuses[i].AllocatedResourcesStatus = append(status.ContainerStatuses[i].AllocatedResourcesStatus, newStatus)\n   836→\t\t\t\t\t// Get pointer to the newly added element *after* appending\n   837→\t\t\t\t\tresStatus = &status.ContainerStatuses[i].AllocatedResourcesStatus[len(status.ContainerStatuses[i].AllocatedResourcesStatus)-1]\n   838→\t\t\t\t\tresourceStatusMap[resourceName] = resStatus\n   839→\t\t\t\t}\n   840→\n   841→\t\t\t\t// Clear previous health entries for this resource before adding current ones\n   842→\t\t\t\t// Ensures we only report current health for allocated devices.\n   843→\t\t\t\tresStatus.Resources = []v1.ResourceHealth{}\n   844→\n   845→\t\t\t\t// Iterate through the map holding the state specific to each driver\n   846→\t\t\t\tfor driverName, driverState := range claimInfo.DriverState {\n   847→\t\t\t\t\t// Iterate through each specific device allocated by this driver\n   848→\t\t\t\t\tfor _, device := range driverState.Devices {\n   849→\n   850→\t\t\t\t\t\thealthStr := m.healthInfoCache.getHealthInfo(driverName, device.PoolName, device.DeviceName)\n   851→\n   852→\t\t\t\t\t\t// Convert internal health string to API type\n   853→\t\t\t\t\t\tvar health v1.ResourceHealthStatus\n   854→\t\t\t\t\t\tswitch healthStr {\n   855→\t\t\t\t\t\tcase \"Healthy\":\n   856→\t\t\t\t\t\t\thealth = v1.ResourceHealthStatusHealthy\n   857→\t\t\t\t\t\tcase \"Unhealthy\":\n   858→\t\t\t\t\t\t\thealth = v1.ResourceHealthStatusUnhealthy\n   859→\t\t\t\t\t\tdefault: // Catches \"Unknown\" or any other case\n   860→\t\t\t\t\t\t\thealth = v1.ResourceHealthStatusUnknown\n   861→\t\t\t\t\t\t}\n   862→\n   863→\t\t\t\t\t\t// Create the ResourceHealth entry\n   864→\t\t\t\t\t\tresourceHealth := v1.ResourceHealth{\n   865→\t\t\t\t\t\t\tHealth: health,\n   866→\t\t\t\t\t\t}\n   867→\n   868→\t\t\t\t\t\t// Use first CDI device ID as ResourceID, with fallback\n   869→\t\t\t\t\t\tif len(device.CDIDeviceIDs) > 0 {\n   870→\t\t\t\t\t\t\tresourceHealth.ResourceID = v1.ResourceID(device.CDIDeviceIDs[0])\n   871→\t\t\t\t\t\t} else {\n   872→\t\t\t\t\t\t\t// Fallback ID if no CDI ID is present\n   873→\t\t\t\t\t\t\tresourceHealth.ResourceID = v1.ResourceID(fmt.Sprintf(\"%s/%s/%s\", driverName, device.PoolName, device.DeviceName))\n   874→\t\t\t\t\t\t}\n   875→\n   876→\t\t\t\t\t\t// Append the health status for this specific device/resource ID\n   877→\t\t\t\t\t\tresStatus.Resources = append(resStatus.Resources, resourceHealth)\n   878→\t\t\t\t\t}\n   879→\t\t\t\t}\n   880→\t\t\t}\n   881→\t\t\t// Rebuild the slice from the map values to ensure correctness\n   882→\t\t\tfinalStatuses := make([]v1.ResourceStatus, 0, len(resourceStatusMap))\n   883→\t\t\tfor _, rs := range resourceStatusMap {\n   884→\t\t\t\t// Only add if it actually has resource health entries populated\n   885→\t\t\t\tif len(rs.Resources) > 0 {\n   886→\t\t\t\t\tfinalStatuses = append(finalStatuses, *rs)\n   887→\t\t\t\t}\n   888→\t\t\t}\n   889→\t\t\tstatus.ContainerStatuses[i].AllocatedResourcesStatus = finalStatuses\n   890→\t\t}\n   891→\t}\n   892→}\n   893→\n   894→// HandleWatchResourcesStream processes health updates from the DRA plugin.\n   895→func (m *Manager) HandleWatchResourcesStream(ctx context.Context, stream drahealthv1alpha1.DRAResourceHealth_NodeWatchResourcesClient, pluginName string) error {\n   896→\tlogger := klog.FromContext(ctx)\n   897→\n   898→\tdefer func() {\n   899→\t\tlogger.V(4).Info(\"Clearing health cache for driver upon stream exit\", \"pluginName\", pluginName)\n   900→\t\t// Use a separate context for clearDriver if needed, though background should be fine.\n   901→\t\tif err := m.healthInfoCache.clearDriver(pluginName); err != nil {\n   902→\t\t\tlogger.Error(err, \"Failed to clear health info cache for driver\", \"pluginName\", pluginName)\n   903→\t\t}\n   904→\t}()\n   905→\n   906→\tfor {\n   907→\t\tresp, err := stream.Recv()\n   908→\t\tif err != nil {\n   909→\t\t\t// Context canceled, normal shutdown.\n   910→\t\t\tif errors.Is(err, context.Canceled) || errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded) {\n   911→\t\t\t\tlogger.V(4).Info(\"Stopping health monitoring due to context cancellation\", \"pluginName\", pluginName, \"reason\", err)\n   912→\t\t\t\treturn err\n   913→\t\t\t}\n   914→\t\t\t// Stream closed cleanly by the server, get normal EOF.\n   915→\t\t\tif errors.Is(err, io.EOF) {\n   916→\t\t\t\tlogger.V(4).Info(\"Stream ended with EOF\", \"pluginName\", pluginName)\n   917→\t\t\t\treturn nil\n   918→\t\t\t}\n   919→\t\t\t// Other errors are unexpected, log & return.\n   920→\t\t\tlogger.Error(err, \"Error receiving from WatchResources stream\", \"pluginName\", pluginName)\n   921→\t\t\treturn err\n   922→\t\t}\n   923→\n   924→\t\t// Convert drahealthv1alpha1.DeviceHealth to state.DeviceHealth\n   925→\t\tdevices := make([]state.DeviceHealth, len(resp.GetDevices()))\n   926→\t\tfor i, d := range resp.GetDevices() {\n   927→\t\t\tvar health state.DeviceHealthStatus\n   928→\t\t\tswitch d.GetHealth() {\n   929→\t\t\tcase drahealthv1alpha1.HealthStatus_HEALTHY:\n   930→\t\t\t\thealth = state.DeviceHealthStatusHealthy\n   931→\t\t\tcase drahealthv1alpha1.HealthStatus_UNHEALTHY:\n   932→\t\t\t\thealth = state.DeviceHealthStatusUnhealthy\n   933→\t\t\tdefault:\n   934→\t\t\t\thealth = state.DeviceHealthStatusUnknown\n   935→\t\t\t}\n   936→\t\t\tdevices[i] = state.DeviceHealth{\n   937→\t\t\t\tPoolName:    d.GetDevice().GetPoolName(),\n   938→\t\t\t\tDeviceName:  d.GetDevice().GetDeviceName(),\n   939→\t\t\t\tHealth:      health,\n   940→\t\t\t\tLastUpdated: time.Unix(d.GetLastUpdatedTime(), 0),\n   941→\t\t\t}\n   942→\t\t}\n   943→\n   944→\t\tchangedDevices, updateErr := m.healthInfoCache.updateHealthInfo(pluginName, devices)\n   945→\t\tif updateErr != nil {\n   946→\t\t\tlogger.Error(updateErr, \"Failed to update health info cache\", \"pluginName\", pluginName)\n devices that are accessible from the given node.\n//\n// Out-dated slices are silently ignored. Pools may be incomplete (not all\n// required slices available) or invalid (for example, device names not unique).\n// Both is recorded in the result.\nfunc GatherPools(ctx context.Context, slices []*resourceapi.ResourceSlice, node *v1.Node, features Features) ([]*Pool, error) {\n\tslicesByPool := make(map[poolIdentifier][]*resourceapi.ResourceSlice)\n\tfor _, slice := range slices {\n\t\tpoolID := poolIdentifier{\n\t\t\tdriver: slice.Spec.Driver,\n\t\t\tpool:   slice.Spec.Pool.Name,\n\t\t}\n\t\tslicesByPool[poolID] = append(slicesByPool[poolID], slice)\n\t}\n\n\t// We need to check whether a pool is complete while we have all\n\t// the slices. We may still be able to allocated from\n\t\t\t// other pools, so we continue.\n\t\t\t//\n\t\t\t// TODO (eventually): let caller decide how to report this to the user. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_017inrNEqJg7cYk9gcKgme9q","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01KbGsPNUpxg9FkBGVmb5New","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/logs/agent/investigation.md","content":"# Investigation Report\n\n## Summary\n\nThe change (commit `2e534d6da10`) modifies `GatherPools()` in all three allocator tiers (stable, incubating, experimental) to compute pool completeness _before_ filtering slices by node affinity, which allows `AllocationMode: All` to succeed for multi-node resource pools where only a subset of the pool's slices target the scheduling node. Previously, such pools were incorrectly marked as incomplete (because the per-node slice count did not match `ResourceSliceCount`), causing allocation to fail with \"pool is currently being updated.\"\n\n## Root Cause\n\nThe scope of the change is narrow in terms of files touched (4 files, `pools_{stable,incubating,experimental}.go` + test), but the behavioral impact is broad: any workload using `AllocationMode: All` with resource pools whose slices span multiple nodes will now successfully allocate, whereas before it was silently blocked. The core bug was in the `IsIncomplete` computation inside `GatherPools()` — it compared `len(pool.Slices)` (post-node-filter) against `pool.Slices[0].Spec.Pool.ResourceSliceCount` (the total across all nodes), which always mismatch for multi-node pools. The fix introduces a pre-filter completeness check using the full set of slices grouped by pool identifier.\n\n### Components affected:\n1. **Structured allocator pool gathering** — the `GatherPools()` function is the direct change site.\n2. **Scheduler DRA plugin Filter path** — `GatherPools()` is called from `Allocator.Allocate()` which is invoked during the `Filter` extension point, the scheduler's hot path evaluated for every node.\n3. **Downstream kubelet/device-plugin behavior** — the kubelet sees different `AllocationResult` contents (potentially more devices from a single pool), but the kubelet code itself is not changed and processes allocation results generically.\n\n## Evidence\n\n### The Commit Diff (2e534d6da10)\n\n**Files changed:**\n- `staging/src/k8s.io/dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/stable/pools_stable.go` (lines 48-95, 140)\n- `staging/src/k8s.io/dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/incubating/pools_incubating.go` (lines 48-95, 140)\n- `staging/src/k8s.io/dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/experimental/pools_experimental.go` (lines 48-95, 162)\n- `staging/src/k8s.io/dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/allocatortesting/allocator_testing.go` (lines 5093-5126)\n\n**Key change in `pools_stable.go`** (identical pattern in incubating/experimental):\n\nBefore (line ~99 of old code):\n```go\npool.IsIncomplete = int64(len(pool.Slices)) != pool.Slices[0].Spec.Pool.ResourceSliceCount\n```\n\nAfter (lines 58-95 new code):\n```go\n// Pre-filter: group ALL slices by pool, check completeness against total slice count and generation\nslicesByPool := make(map[poolIdentifier][]*resourceapi.ResourceSlice)\n// ... grouping logic ...\nincompletePools := sets.New[poolIdentifier]()\n// ... completeness check using full (unfiltered) slice set ...\n\n// Then later at line 143:\npool.IsIncomplete = incompletePools.Has(poolIdentifier{driver: pool.Driver.String(), pool: pool.Pool.String()})\n```\n\n### Allocation Logic That Depends on `IsIncomplete`\n\n**`allocator_stable.go:402-417`** — The `AllocationMode: All` handler iterates all pools and **rejects** the entire allocation if any pool is incomplete:\n```go\ncase resourceapi.DeviceAllocationModeAll:\n    for _, pool := range pools {\n        if pool.IsIncomplete {\n            return requestData, fmt.Errorf(\"...asks for all devices, but resource pool %s is currently being updated...\")\n        }\n        if pool.IsInvalid {\n            return requestData, fmt.Errorf(\"...asks for all devices, but resource pool %s is currently invalid...\")\n        }\n        // ... enumerate selectable devices from pool ...\n    }\n```\n\nThis means the `IsIncomplete` flag is the gatekeeper for `AllocationMode: All`. The old code's false-positive `IsIncomplete` for multi-node pools was the root blocker.\n\n### Scheduler Hot Path Tracing\n\n1. **PreFilter** (`dynamicresources.go:529`) — Creates the `Allocator` instance, stores it in cycle state.\n2. **Filter** (`dynamicresources.go:884`) — For each candidate node, calls `state.allocator.Allocate(ctx, node, claims)` at line 964.\n3. **`Allocate()`** (`allocator_stable.go:97`) — Calls `GatherPools()` at line 114 (the changed function), then runs the recursive backtracking allocator via `allocateOne()`.\n4. **`allocateOne()`** (`allocator_stable.go:681`) — For `AllocationMode: All`, iterates `requestData.allDevices` (line 765-778), which was populated during pool gathering.\n\n### Pool Gathering Call Chain\n\n```\nFilter() [dynamicresources.go:964]\n  -> Allocator.Allocate() [allocator_stable.go:97]\n    -> GatherPools() [pools_stable.go:58]  <-- THE CHANGED FUNCTION\n      -> nodeMatches() per slice\n      -> addSlice() per matching slice\n      -> incompletePools check (NEW: pre-filter completeness)\n    -> prepareRequest() [allocator_stable.go:364]\n      -> checks pool.IsIncomplete for AllocationMode:All\n    -> allocateOne() [allocator_stable.go:681]\n```\n\n### API Definitions for AllocationMode\n\n- **v1**: `staging/src/k8s.io/api/resource/v1/types.go:893` — `AllocationMode DeviceAllocationMode` field on `ExactDeviceRequest`\n- **v1**: `staging/src/k8s.io/api/resource/v1/types.go:1021` — `AllocationMode DeviceAllocationMode` field on `DeviceSubRequest`\n- **v1**: `staging/src/k8s.io/api/resource/v1/types.go:1112-1113` — Constants: `DeviceAllocationModeExactCount`, `DeviceAllocationModeAll`\n- **v1beta1**: `staging/src/k8s.io/api/resource/v1beta1/types.go:864`\n- **v1beta2**: `staging/src/k8s.io/api/resource/v1beta2/types.go:900`\n\n### Validation\n\n- `pkg/apis/resource/validation/validation.go:268-286` — `validateDeviceAllocationMode()`: for `All` mode, Count must be 0; for `ExactCount`, Count must be > 0.\n\n### Test Coverage\n\nNew test case added in `allocatortesting/allocator_testing.go:5093-5126`:\n- Test name: `\"allocation-mode-all-with-multi-host-resource-pool\"`\n- Creates a pool with 2 slices on different nodes (`node1`, `node2`), `ResourceSliceCount=2`\n- Claims `AllocationMode: All` from class A\n- Expects allocation to succeed on node1 with only device1 (the device from node1's slice)\n\n## Affected Components\n\n### HIGH RISK\n\n| Package | File(s) | Impact |\n|---------|---------|--------|\n| `dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/stable` | `pools_stable.go:58-149` | **Direct change site.** `GatherPools()` runs on every `Filter()` call for every node in the scheduling cycle. Extra map allocation + iteration adds overhead proportional to total ResourceSlice count. |\n| `dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/incubating` | `pools_incubating.go:58-140` | Same change, incubating allocator tier. |\n| `dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/experimental` | `pools_experimental.go:58-162` | Same change, experimental allocator tier. |\n| `scheduler/framework/plugins/dynamicresources` | `dynamicresources.go:884-1019` | **Filter() hot path.** Calls `Allocate()` which calls `GatherPools()`. Any regression here affects scheduling latency for all pods with DRA claims. Filter already has an optional timeout (`EnableDRASchedulerFilterTimeout`). |\n\n### MEDIUM RISK\n\n| Package | File(s) | Impact |\n|---------|---------|--------|\n| `dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/stable` | `allocator_stable.go:402-437` | The `AllocationMode: All` handler now sees multi-node pools as complete and enumerates devices across them. Previously unreachable code path for multi-node pools is now reachable — the `isSelectable()` and `allocateDevice()` paths will process more devices. |\n| `kubelet/cm/dra` | `manager.go:226-473` | **Kubelet PrepareResources.** The `AllocationResult` from the scheduler may now reference devices from a multi-node pool. The kubelet processes results generically (groups by driver, calls `NodePrepareResources` gRPC), so the code path is unchanged, but the _data_ is different. A device listed in the allocation result must be physically present on the node for the DRA driver to prepare it. |\n| `kubelet/cm/dra` | `claiminfo.go:57-79` | `newClaimInfoFromClaim()` parses `AllocationResult.Devices.Results` — will now see results from multi-node pool allocations. No code change needed, but more device entries per claim are possible. |\n| `pkg/apis/resource/validation` | `validation.go:268-286` | No change, but validation allows `AllocationMode: All` with Count=0. If multi-node pools produce allocations with more devices than expected, the `AllocationResultsMaxSize` check in `allocator_stable.go:758` is the only guard. |\n| `pkg/quota/v1/evaluator/core` | `resource_claims_test.go` | Quota evaluation counts devices per claim. More devices from multi-node `All` mode means higher quota consumption. |\n\n### LOW RISK\n\n| Package | File(s) | Impact |\n|---------|---------|--------|\n| `pkg/apis/resource/v1/defaults.go` | Lines 31-48 | Defaults `AllocationMode` to `ExactCount` — unaffected by this change. |\n| `pkg/apis/resource/v1beta1/conversion.go` | Lines 49-93 | API version conversion — passes `AllocationMode` through unchanged. |\n| `pkg/apis/resource/v1beta2/conversion.go` | Auto-generated | Same. |\n| `client-go applyconfigurations` | `resource/v1/devicerequest.go` etc. | Client-side builders — no behavioral change. |\n| `pkg/printers/internalversion` | `printers_test.go` | Display logic — no behavioral change. |\n| `pkg/registry/resource/resourceclaim` | `strategy.go`, `declarative_validation_test.go` | API server storage strategy — validates and stores claims, no allocation logic. |\n\n## Recommendation\n\n### Risk Mitigation Strategy\n\n1. **The `IsIncomplete` false-positive was the only thing preventing `AllocationMode: All` from working with multi-node pools.** The fix is semantically correct: completeness should be determined from the full set of slices, not the post-node-filter subset. However, the previous behavior served as an accidental safety net — it prevented allocating \"all\" devices from a pool where the scheduler only sees a subset of slices.\n\n2. **Key invariant to verify:** When `AllocationMode: All` succeeds on a node, the allocation result must only contain devices whose slices match that node. The existing code in `allocator_stable.go:419-434` iterates `pool.Slices` (which are already node-filtered by `GatherPools`), so this invariant holds — the fix does not change which slices are in a pool after node filtering, only whether the pool is marked incomplete.\n\n3. **Performance concern:** `GatherPools()` now does two passes over all slices (first to compute completeness, then to filter by node). For clusters with many ResourceSlices (e.g., 1000+ GPUs across 100 nodes), this doubles the iteration in the pool gathering phase. This runs inside the `Filter` extension point for every node candidate. Mitigation: the scheduler already has `EnableDRASchedulerFilterTimeout` and the allocator is scoped to claims for the current pod, limiting the slice set.\n\n4. **The existence of a revert commit (`a0e500ace39`)** suggests this change was previously landed and then reverted, indicating prior issues were encountered. The current HEAD has the change re-applied. The revert/re-apply history warrants extra scrutiny.\n\n### Testing Plan\n\n1. **Unit tests (existing + new):**\n   - New test `\"allocation-mode-all-with-multi-host-resource-pool\"` in `allocatortesting/allocator_testing.go:5093` covers the core scenario.\n   - Existing tests for `AllocationMode: All` in single-node scenarios remain in `allocator_testing.go` (search for `DeviceAllocationModeAll`).\n   - Run: `go test ./staging/src/k8s.io/dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/...`\n\n2. **Integration tests:**\n   - `test/integration/dra/dra_test.go:223` — `TestDRA` covers end-to-end DRA scheduling.\n   - `test/integration/scheduler_perf/dra/dra_test.go:41` — Performance benchmarks.\n   - Run: `go test ./test/integration/dra/...`\n\n3. **E2E tests:**\n   - `test/e2e/dra/dra.go` — Extensive allocation tests including multi-node scenarios (\"must allow multiple allocations and consume capacity\" at line 1899).\n\n4. **Recommended additional tests:**\n   - **Multi-node pool with incomplete slices + `AllocationMode: All`:** Verify the scheduler correctly blocks allocation when a multi-node pool is genuinely incomplete (generation mismatch or slice count mismatch).\n   - **Multi-node pool with `AllocationMode: All` and device selectors:** Ensure CEL selector evaluation works correctly when the pool spans nodes but only node-local devices are enumerated.\n   - **Quota impact test:** Verify that `AllocationMode: All` from a multi-node pool with N devices on the local node correctly reports N device allocations for quota purposes (not the total across all nodes).\n   - **Scale test:** Benchmark `GatherPools()` with 500+ ResourceSlices across 50+ pools to measure the overhead of the double-pass iteration.\n   - **Concurrent scheduling test:** Two pods requesting `AllocationMode: All` from the same multi-node pool should not double-allocate devices — the `deviceInUse()` check at `allocator_stable.go:1205` and `allocatedDevices` tracking should prevent this, but it needs validation with multi-node pools specifically.\n\n5. **Risk of regression if the change has bugs:**\n   - **False-negative `IsIncomplete`:** If the pre-filter completeness check has a bug (e.g., slice grouping by wrong key), a genuinely incomplete pool could be marked complete, leading `AllocationMode: All` to allocate a partial set of devices. The allocation would succeed but miss devices still being registered.\n   - **Scheduler crash/panic:** If `slices[0]` is accessed on an empty slice group in the completeness check (line 75 in the new code: `generation := slices[0].Spec.Pool.Generation`), this would panic. This is guarded by the map construction which only creates entries when slices exist, but deserves a defensive check.\n   - **Kubelet failure:** If the allocation result includes a device not physically available on the node, the DRA driver's `NodePrepareResources` gRPC call will fail, causing the pod to enter `ContainerCreating` state indefinitely. Previously, such pools were incorrectly marked as incomplete (because the per-node slice count did not match `ResourceSliceCount`), causing allocation to fail with \"pool is currently being updated.\"\n\n## Root Cause\n\nThe scope of the change is narrow in terms of files touched (4 files, `pools_{stable,incubating,experimental}.go` + test), but the behavioral impact is broad: any workload using `AllocationMode: All` with resource pools whose slices span multiple nodes will now successfully allocate, whereas before it was silently blocked. The core bug was in the `IsIncomplete` computation inside `GatherPools()` — it compared `len(pool.Slices)` (post-node-filter) against `pool.Slices[0].Spec.Pool.ResourceSliceCount` (the total across all nodes), which always mismatch for multi-node pools. The fix introduces a pre-filter completeness check using the full set of slices grouped by pool identifier.\n\n### Components affected:\n1. **Structured allocator pool gathering** — the `GatherPools()` function is the direct change site.\n2. **Scheduler DRA plugin Filter path** — `GatherPools()` is called from `Allocator.Allocate()` which is invoked during the `Filter` extension point, the scheduler's hot path evaluated for every node.\n3. **Downstream kubelet/device-plugin behavior** — the kubelet sees different `AllocationResult` contents (potentially more devices from a single pool), but the kubelet code itself is not changed and processes allocation results generically.\n\n## Evidence\n\n### The Commit Diff (2e534d6da10)\n\n**Files changed:**\n- `staging/src/k8s.io/dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/stable/pools_stable.go` (lines 48-95, 140)\n- `staging/src/k8s.io/dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/incubating/pools_incubating.go` (lines 48-95, 140)\n- `staging/src/k8s.io/dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/experimental/pools_experimental.go` (lines 48-95, 162)\n- `staging/src/k8s.io/dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/allocatortesting/allocator_testing.go` (lines 5093-5126)\n\n**Key change in `pools_stable.go`** (identical pattern in incubating/experimental):\n\nBefore (line ~99 of old code):\n```go\npool.IsIncomplete = int64(len(pool.Slices)) != pool.Slices[0].Spec.Pool.ResourceSliceCount\n```\n\nAfter (lines 58-95 new code):\n```go\n// Pre-filter: group ALL slices by pool, check completeness against total slice count and generation\nslicesByPool := make(map[poolIdentifier][]*resourceapi.ResourceSlice)\n// ... grouping logic ...\nincompletePools := sets.New[poolIdentifier]()\n// ... completeness check using full (unfiltered) slice set ...\n\n// Then later at line 143:\npool.IsIncomplete = incompletePools.Has(poolIdentifier{driver: pool.Driver.String(), pool: pool.Pool.String()})\n```\n\n### Allocation Logic That Depends on `IsIncomplete`\n\n**`allocator_stable.go:402-417`** — The `AllocationMode: All` handler iterates all pools and **rejects** the entire allocation if any pool is incomplete:\n```go\ncase resourceapi.DeviceAllocationModeAll:\n    for _, pool := range pools {\n        if pool.IsIncomplete {\n            return requestData, fmt.Errorf(\"...asks for all devices, but resource pool %s is currently being updated...\")\n        }\n        if pool.IsInvalid {\n            return requestData, fmt.Errorf(\"...asks for all devices, but resource pool %s is currently invalid...\")\n        }\n        // ... enumerate selectable devices from pool ...\n    }\n```\n\nThis means the `IsIncomplete` flag is the gatekeeper for `AllocationMode: All`. The old code's false-positive `IsIncomplete` for multi-node pools was the root blocker.\n\n### Scheduler Hot Path Tracing\n\n1. **PreFilter** (`dynamicresources.go:529`) — Creates the `Allocator` instance, stores it in cycle state.\n2. **Filter** (`dynamicresources.go:884`) — For each candidate node, calls `state.allocator.Allocate(ctx, node, claims)` at line 964.\n3. **`Allocate()`** (`allocator_stable.go:97`) — Calls `GatherPools()` at line 114 (the changed function), then runs the recursive backtracking allocator via `allocateOne()`.\n4. **`allocateOne()`** (`allocator_stable.go:681`) — For `AllocationMode: All`, iterates `requestData.allDevices` (line 765-778), which was populated during pool gathering.\n\n### Pool Gathering Call Chain\n\n```\nFilter() [dynamicresources.go:964]\n  -> Allocator.Allocate() [allocator_stable.go:97]\n    -> GatherPools() [pools_stable.go:58]  <-- THE CHANGED FUNCTION\n      -> nodeMatches() per slice\n      -> addSlice() per matching slice\n      -> incompletePools check (NEW: pre-filter completeness)\n    -> prepareRequest() [allocator_stable.go:364]\n      -> checks pool.IsIncomplete for AllocationMode:All\n    -> allocateOne() [allocator_stable.go:681]\n```\n\n### API Definitions for AllocationMode\n\n- **v1**: `staging/src/k8s.io/api/resource/v1/types.go:893` — `AllocationMode DeviceAllocationMode` field on `ExactDeviceRequest`\n- **v1**: `staging/src/k8s.io/api/resource/v1/types.go:1021` — `AllocationMode DeviceAllocationMode` field on `DeviceSubRequest`\n- **v1**: `staging/src/k8s.io/api/resource/v1/types.go:1112-1113` — Constants: `DeviceAllocationModeExactCount`, `DeviceAllocationModeAll`\n- **v1beta1**: `staging/src/k8s.io/api/resource/v1beta1/types.go:864`\n- **v1beta2**: `staging/src/k8s.io/api/resource/v1beta2/types.go:900`\n\n### Validation\n\n- `pkg/apis/resource/validation/validation.go:268-286` — `validateDeviceAllocationMode()`: for `All` mode, Count must be 0; for `ExactCount`, Count must be > 0.\n\n### Test Coverage\n\nNew test case added in `allocatortesting/allocator_testing.go:5093-5126`:\n- Test name: `\"allocation-mode-all-with-multi-host-resource-pool\"`\n- Creates a pool with 2 slices on different nodes (`node1`, `node2`), `ResourceSliceCount=2`\n- Claims `AllocationMode: All` from class A\n- Expects allocation to succeed on node1 with only device1 (the device from node1's slice)\n\n## Affected Components\n\n### HIGH RISK\n\n| Package | File(s) | Impact |\n|---------|---------|--------|\n| `dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/stable` | `pools_stable.go:58-149` | **Direct change site.** `GatherPools()` runs on every `Filter()` call for every node in the scheduling cycle. Extra map allocation + iteration adds overhead proportional to total ResourceSlice count. |\n| `dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/incubating` | `pools_incubating.go:58-140` | Same change, incubating allocator tier. |\n| `dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/experimental` | `pools_experimental.go:58-162` | Same change, experimental allocator tier. |\n| `scheduler/framework/plugins/dynamicresources` | `dynamicresources.go:884-1019` | **Filter() hot path.** Calls `Allocate()` which calls `GatherPools()`. Filter already has an optional timeout (`EnableDRASchedulerFilterTimeout`). |\n\n### MEDIUM RISK\n\n| Package | File(s) | Impact |\n|---------|---------|--------|\n| `dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/stable` | `allocator_stable.go:402-437` | The `AllocationMode: All` handler now sees multi-node pools as complete and enumerates devices across them. Previously unreachable code path for multi-node pools is now reachable — the `isSelectable()` and `allocateDevice()` paths will process more devices. |\n| `kubelet/cm/dra` | `manager.go:226-473` | **Kubelet PrepareResources.** The `AllocationResult` from the scheduler may now reference devices from a multi-node pool. The kubelet processes results generically (groups by driver, calls `NodePrepareResources` gRPC), so the code path is unchanged, but the _data_ is different. A device listed in the allocation result must be physically present on the node for the DRA driver to prepare it. |\n| `kubelet/cm/dra` | `claiminfo.go:57-79` | `newClaimInfoFromClaim()` parses `AllocationResult.Devices.Results` — will now see results from multi-node pool allocations. If multi-node pools produce allocations with more devices than expected, the `AllocationResultsMaxSize` check in `allocator_stable.go:758` is the only guard. |\n| `pkg/quota/v1/evaluator/core` | `resource_claims_test.go` | Quota evaluation counts devices per claim. More devices from multi-node `All` mode means higher quota consumption. |\n\n### LOW RISK\n\n| Package | File(s) | Impact |\n|---------|---------|--------|\n| `pkg/apis/resource/v1/defaults.go` | Lines 31-48 | Defaults `AllocationMode` to `ExactCount` — unaffected by this change. |\n| `pkg/apis/resource/v1beta1/conversion.go` | Lines 49-93 | API version conversion — passes `AllocationMode` through unchanged. |\n| `pkg/apis/resource/v1beta2/conversion.go` | Auto-generated | Same. |\n| `client-go applyconfigurations` | `resource/v1/devicerequest.go` etc. | Client-side builders — no behavioral change. |\n| `pkg/printers/internalversion` | `printers_test.go` | Display logic — no behavioral change. |\n| `pkg/registry/resource/resourceclaim` | `strategy.go`, `declarative_validation_test.go` | API server storage strategy — validates and stores claims, no allocation logic. |\n\n## Recommendation\n\n### Risk Mitigation Strategy\n\n1. **The `IsIncomplete` false-positive was the only thing preventing `AllocationMode: All` from working with multi-node pools.** The fix is semantically correct: completeness should be determined from the full set of slices, not the post-node-filter subset. However, the previous behavior served as an accidental safety net — it prevented allocating \"all\" devices from a pool where the scheduler only sees a subset of slices.\n\n2. **Key invariant to verify:** When `AllocationMode: All` succeeds on a node, the allocation result must only contain devices whose slices match that node. The existing code in `allocator_stable.go:419-434` iterates `pool.Slices` (which are already node-filtered by `GatherPools`), so this invariant holds — the fix does not change which slices are in a pool after node filtering, only whether the pool is marked incomplete.\n\n3. **Performance concern:** `GatherPools()` now does two passes over all slices (first to compute completeness, then to filter by node). For clusters with many ResourceSlices (e.g., 1000+ GPUs across 100 nodes), this doubles the iteration in the pool gathering phase. This runs inside the `Filter` extension point for every node candidate. Mitigation: the scheduler already has `EnableDRASchedulerFilterTimeout` and the allocator is scoped to claims for the current pod, limiting the slice set.\n\n4. **The existence of a revert commit (`a0e500ace39`)** suggests this change was previously landed and then reverted, indicating prior issues were encountered. The current HEAD has the change re-applied. The revert/re-apply history warrants extra scrutiny.\n\n### Testing Plan\n\n1. **Unit tests (existing + new):**\n   - New test `\"allocation-mode-all-with-multi-host-resource-pool\"` in `allocatortesting/allocator_testing.go:5093` covers the core scenario.\n   - Existing tests for `AllocationMode: All` in single-node scenarios remain in `allocator_testing.go` (search for `DeviceAllocationModeAll`).\n   - Run: `go test ./staging/src/k8s.io/dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/...`\n\n2. **Integration tests:**\n   - `test/integration/dra/dra_test.go:223` — `TestDRA` covers end-to-end DRA scheduling.\n   - `test/integration/scheduler_perf/dra/dra_test.go:41` — Performance benchmarks.\n   - Run: `go test ./test/integration/dra/...`\n\n3. **E2E tests:**\n   - `test/e2e/dra/dra.go` — Extensive allocation tests including multi-node scenarios (\"must allow multiple allocations and consume capacity\" at line 1899).\n\n4. **Recommended additional tests:**\n   - **Multi-node pool with incomplete slices + `AllocationMode: All`:** Verify the scheduler correctly blocks allocation when a multi-node pool is genuinely incomplete (generation mismatch or slice count mismatch).\n   - **Multi-node pool with `AllocationMode: All` and device selectors:** Ensure CEL selector evaluation works correctly when the pool spans nodes but only node-local devices are enumerated.\n   - **Quota impact test:** Verify that `AllocationMode: All` from a multi-node pool with N devices on the local node correctly reports N device allocations for quota purposes (not the total across all nodes).\n   - **Scale test:** Benchmark `GatherPools()` with 500+ ResourceSlices across 50+ pools to measure the overhead of the double-pass iteration.\n   - **Concurrent scheduling test:** Two pods requesting `AllocationMode: All` from the same multi-node pool should not double-allocate devices — the `deviceInUse()` check at `allocator_stable.go:1205` and `allocatedDevices` tracking should prevent this, but it needs validation with multi-node pools specifically.\n\n5. **Risk of regression if the change has bugs:**\n   - **False-negative `IsIncomplete`:** If the pre-filter completeness check has a bug (e.g., slice grouping by wrong key), a genuinely incomplete pool could be marked complete, leading `AllocationMode: All` to allocate a partial set of devices. The allocation would succeed but miss devices still being registered.\n   - **Scheduler crash/panic:** If `slices[0]` is accessed on an empty slice group in the completeness check (line 75 in the new code: `generation := slices[0].Spec.Pool.Generation`), this would panic. This is guarded by the map construction which only creates entries when slices exist, but deserves a defensive check.\n   - **Kubelet failure:** If the allocation result includes a device not physically available on the node, the DRA driver's `NodePrepareResources` gRPC call will fail, causing the pod to enter `ContainerCreating` state indefinitely. This would be the most user-visible failure mode.\n   - **Performance degradation at scale:** The extra map allocation and iteration in `GatherPools()` could measurably increase scheduling latency in clusters with thousands of ResourceSlices, since this runs in the Filter hot path for every candidate node.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
